## 1. Purpose, scope and rules

**Purpose.** Plan the first features the iOS app does not have yet, taken from the "Windows features without an iOS counterpart" list in `PORTING_BLOCKERS.md` (section "Windows features without an iOS counterpart") and `PORTING_PLAN.md` section 5, in the owner's order and nothing else:

| Milestone | Features |
|---|---|
| M1 authoring basics | New Condition, condition folders, layers (create, rename, delete, reorder as Windows does), Set Scale, rename page, delete page |
| M2 placement tools | clipboard / paste, backout (hole) modes, snap to PDF lines and takeoff endpoints |
| M3 exports | Summary CSV, OST and OSP export (round trip: an iOS export re-opens on Windows), PDF export |
| M4 annotations and views | annotations and hotlinks, image modes (bitonal, invert, adjust), overlay image drawing, 3D section cuts, whole-scene edge overlay |

Out of scope (the owner's list, section 10): SQL Server collaboration, AI takeoff, payroll / employees / job statuses / cover sheet / bid areas, the tower crane, detached windows, FBX. There is no M5.

**Standing rules of this work** (they apply to every section below):

1. **Windows is the source of truth for all logic** (math, validation, ordering, naming rules, persistence, defaults). Only the UI is adapted for iOS (touch, iPhone and iPad).
2. **A Windows behaviour that looks accidental, inconsistent or unsafe is NOT copied.** The safe behaviour is kept and the case is listed for the owner with the Windows file:line (section 8, and section 7 where the owner must choose). This OVERRIDES the earlier rule "port Windows as is and list possible Windows defects" (`PORTING_PLAN.md` section 7, the bullet "Rule for any further parity work", line 115 of the working tree this plan was written against; the rule since job 6) for this new work, by the owner's request.
3. **Logic goes in the pure C++ Core with host tests** (`Core/`, tests in `Config/Tests/`); the Objective-C(++) layer stays thin (UIKit, gestures, presentation, one-line bridges in `Services/`).
4. **Tests first**: port the Windows tests with the same inputs and expected outputs, add data-safety cases, watch the new tests fail for the right reason, then implement. Never relax a tolerance (`Config/Tests/README.md`, "Add a test"). After the code, **an independent reviewer who did not write the change** checks each feature against the Windows code (the cited lines), and the findings are fixed before the milestone is called ready.
5. **One branch per milestone**: `ios-feature-m1` from the updated `main`, then `ios-feature-m2` from `main` after M1 was merged, and so on. Logical commits (tests, Core, store, bridge, UI, docs). (`PORTING_PLAN.md` section 3 already names `ios-feature-m1` as the branch of the next device pack; if that branch exists when M1 starts, M1 continues on it after it is brought up to the updated `main`.) A push happens only at a green point because `build.yml` runs on every push. GitHub "Build IPA" green, the IPA checked, a DeviceTestPack and device checks (`DEVICE_CHECKS.md`) per milestone.
6. **Work stops after each milestone** until the owner sends the device results. Merge into `main` only when every check of that milestone's pack is recorded PASS (a blank or failed check blocks the merge: the rule of job 7); an open item that the plan marks as closed in a later milestone (OI-M1-1, 6.4) is not a check of the pack.
7. **Windows round trip is only provable from M3.** M1 and M2 write into the iOS SQLite database; whether Windows accepts those rows is guarded by host tests and by Windows-schema reading until the OST/OSP export (M3) exists. M3 must therefore round-trip everything M1 and M2 create.

## 2. Starting point

### 2.1 What the iOS app does today

A viewer and editor of imported bids: it imports `.ost` and `.osp` (`Config/Info.plist`, `Services/ImportService.mm`), browses projects and bids (`Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm`, folder / trash / move rules in `Core/Domain/BrowserRules.*`), opens a bid in 2D (PDF page via CGPDF, takeoff overlay: `PlanView/`) and 3D (bgfx/Metal: `Rendering/`), places takeoffs (area, linear, count; grid and angle snap only), drags and edits them, deletes, undoes (50 entries; Move, MoveGroup, Place, Delete, SetNegative: `Controllers/ViewController/Components/UndoRedoController.mm:7`), edits a condition (`update_condition`, `Controllers/EditConditionViewController/`), duplicates and deletes a condition, toggles layer visibility (single, Show All / Hide All: `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Layers.mm`), shows quantities, and exports OBJ, DXF and HTML through the share sheet (`Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Export.mm:5-40`).

### 2.2 What it cannot do (the plan's subject)

(The state before M1. **Updated 2026-11-24 (job 8, M1):** M1 built New Condition, condition folders, layers, Set Scale, rename page, delete page (5.1-5.6), the bid lock guard (4.1) and the popover anchors (4.3); the other bullets are still true for M2 to M4.)

- **No creation or structure edit**: no New Condition, no condition folders (read-only tree: `Core/Domain/ConditionTree.cpp`), no layer create / rename / delete / reorder.
- **No page write at all**: no `UPDATE BidPages` anywhere in `Core/`, `Services/`, `Controllers/` (grep, 0 hits). No Set Scale, rename, delete.
- **No bid lock guard**: `JobStatuses.Locked` is only declared in the schema (`Core/Persistence/OstSchema.cpp:721`, `:1136`) and carried by the import, which reconciles `JobStatuses` by Name and remaps `Bids.JobStatusUID` (`ProjectStore+ImportMasterData.cpp:196`, `:322-326`, `OstImportTransform.cpp:151-153`); nothing reads it for locking.
- No clipboard, no multi-selection, no paste history entry, no backout mode, no PDF-line / endpoint snap (`Core/Geometry/placement_snap.hpp:44-48`: `SnapKind` is only `None` / `Grid`).
- No Summary CSV, OST, OSP or PDF export; no CAB writer; no raw-row reader.
- No annotation model, no hotlinks, no image modes, no overlay drawing (loaded, never drawn), no 3D cuts, no feature-edge overlay (only the yellow selection outline of the selected mesh: feature edges are extracted per mesh at `Rendering/Renderer.mm:413` and drawn yellow at `:736-757`).
- No popover anchor on the seven action sheets of the Edit Condition pickers (`Controllers/EditConditionViewController/Components/EditConditionViewController+Pickers.mm:54-287` (the seven sheets at :58, :81, :116, :151, :175, :228, :269); owner note m in `PORTING_BLOCKERS.md`): UIKit throws on iPad for an action sheet without an anchor (the crash itself was not observed here). [Updated 2026-11-24 (job 8, M1): S0 (54ff58e) added a popoverPresentationController anchor to each of the seven pickers; the iPad behaviour is device check M1-01, not run.]

(Updated 2026-11-27: after parity pass 2 the count is 2938 host tests, 2947 with Manifold; the M1 build ended at 2907; after parity pass 3 it is 2975, 2987 with Manifold.) Statuses 176 / 23 / 16 / 4 / 1 = 220 (after parity pass 3: 176 / 23 / 0 / 13 / 1 / 7: EXP-13, EXP-M02 and GEO-15 moved from wont-fix to no-longer-divergent). Windows `49a2d59` (the current Windows reference is `d1c430a`, see the updates above). GitHub run 44953904172 (head `e151655`) built the last code of the parity job; the owner ran that IPA on a phone (DEV-01 to DEV-11 PASS). The baseline for this plan is `main` at the merge of `ios-parity-sync` (merge commit `06d8d69`, 2026-11-22; the merge was not done when this file was first written; the `main` CI run 45039125909 succeeded).

## 3. Milestone overview

| Milestone | Features (size, risk) | Overall size / risk | Order of work | Blocks / depends |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** authoring basics | New Condition (M, low-medium); condition folders (M, low-medium); layers (M, medium-low); Set Scale (L, high for data); rename page (S, low); delete page (M, high consequence) | L / medium-high | foundations (lock guard with its one UI bridge, UID limit, text-limit helpers, anchor fix) -> four streams in parallel -> integration, CI, IPA, device pack | blocks M2 (allocator, page-scale rescale, lock guard, undo capture), M3 (rows to round-trip), M4 (cascades, rect rescale) |
| **M2** placement tools | clipboard / paste (L, medium); backout modes (M, medium); snap (L, medium-high) | L / medium-high | clipboard Core + store first, backout mode in parallel, Paste Backout after both, snap in parallel (PDF extractor last); file ownership of the three M2 streams is in 6.5 | depends on M1 (allocator `count > 1`, `PageScaleTransform`, lock guard, page menu); feeds M3 (pasted rows, holes), M4 (snap resolver for annotation placement) |
| **M3** exports | Summary CSV (M, medium); OST + OSP export (L, high); PDF export (L, high until the QPDF spike passes) | XL / high | CSV first (builds strip / unescape / share plumbing), OST + OSP next, PDF after the QPDF spike | depends on M1 and M2 rows for round-trip tests; feeds M4 (annotation value forms, latin-1 rule, PDF extension) |
| **M4** annotations and views | annotations + hotlinks, named views as the hotlink target (L, high); image modes (M, medium); overlay image (L, medium); 3D section cuts (L, medium); edge overlay (S-M, medium) | XL / high | page-write features first (image modes), overlay, annotations (read-only drawing, then edit waves), 3D: edge overlay before cuts | depends on M1 (Set Scale and Delete Page cascades must already cover annotation / named-view / hotlink tables; overlay rect rescale), M2 (snap resolver, clipboard remap), M3 (export keeps all value forms) |

### Dependency graph between milestones

```
M1 foundations ---------------------------------------------------------------+
  bid lock guard (ProjectStore+BidLock)      -> every later writer (M2 paste/hole, M4 annotations/overlay/image modes)
  UID limit 2147483647 + 64-bit allocator    -> M1 writers; M2 paste (allocate count>1); M3 export fits Access Long
  allocate_condition_identities(count)       -> M2 cross-bid paste (conditions cloned)
  PageScaleTransform (Set Scale)             -> M2 paste redo; M4 annotations (rotation slot), M4 overlay rect
  undo entry carries the page scale          -> M2 Paste entry; M4 typed annotation entries
  page-write pattern (bid scope, one transaction, lock) -> M4 image-mode and overlay writes (no new helper)
  rows M1 creates (conditions, folders, layers, scale, names, deletes) -> M3 OST/OSP must round-trip them
M2 -> M3: pasted rows, extras with regenerated GUID, holes + parent links export and re-import
M2 snap resolver -> M4 annotation placement (hotlink point, line / arrow / dimension, polygon / cloud)
M3 -> M4: annotation tables written with the Windows value forms (rotation index kept, Latin-1 text),
         PDF export extension (annotation families, overlay background) lands in M4
M3 Summary CSV -> M3 OST/OSP: strip_elevation_suffix, collision note, share-sheet plumbing
```

Hard order: M1 -> M2 -> M3 -> M4, each on its own branch from the updated `main`, each stopped until the owner's device results. Inside a milestone only the "Order of work" of the feature sections (5.x) and section 6 apply.

Estimated new host tests (planning figures, not measured): M1 +410 to +490 (the sum of the feature estimates, 385 to 455, plus about 25 to 35 for S0: the lock guard cases of 4.1, the UID-limit cases of 4.2 and the text-limit cases of 6.2 S0.b; as built: +1147, 1760 -> 2907, S0 alone +97, well above the estimate; the feature chains also added review and mutation-survivor tests: 9a.2), M2 +240 to +300 (parity pass 2, Windows d26893c: the estimate holds, read as the upper half, about +280 to +300; 5.7 grows from about 100 to 110-120 host cases, plus about 3 to 5 for the negative group wiring: still inside the estimate), M3 +450 to +500, M4 +500 to +550 (counts from the "portable Windows tests" of each feature plus data-safety cases).

## 4. Cross-cutting foundations

Several features need the same piece. For each piece ONE design is chosen here (where the research results proposed different helpers, the chosen one is named and the others are dropped), the milestone that builds it is named, and the later users reuse it.

### 4.1 Bid lock guard (built in M1, step 0)

- **Windows**: a bid is locked when its job status is Locked; no job status = unlocked; a status that cannot be resolved = locked (fail closed): `ost_visualizer/domain/entities/cover_sheet.py:15-23` (`resolve_bid_status_lock`), `ost_visualizer/application/services/project_read_service.py:81-94` (`is_bid_locked`, unresolved -> locked, logged). The write services refuse a write on the ACTIVE locked bid: `application/services/active_bid_write_guard.py:7-36`, called e.g. at `project_write_service.py:6616` (insert layer) and `:6800` (swap layers). Create / rename / delete / Set Scale all sit behind it (UI gate: `presentation/managers/ui_access_manager.py:69-236`, `:711-783`).
- **iOS today**: none (section 2.2).
- **Chosen design (one)**: pure Domain `Core/Domain/BidLock.hpp/.cpp` (`ost_domain::resolve_bid_status_lock(statuses, status_uid)` returning true / false / unresolved) and store functions in `Core/Persistence/ProjectStore/Components/ProjectStore+BidLock.cpp`: `bool ost_store::integrity::bid_is_locked(ost_db::OstDatabase& db, long long bid_uid, bool& locked)`, used INSIDE every writer's transaction on the same connection (a db_path function would open a second connection while the writer holds `BEGIN IMMEDIATE`, `OstDatabase.hpp:118`), and the wrapper `bool ost_store::bid_is_locked(const std::string& db_path, const std::string& bid_uid, bool& locked)`, which opens its own connection and is used only by the UI to show the message before an action (the existing pattern: public db_path functions in `ost_store` over `OstDatabase&` helpers in `ost_store::integrity`, `ProjectStore_Integrity.hpp:20-39`). Both return false on a failed read and set `locked = true` (fail closed). They read `Bids.JobStatusUID` -> `JobStatuses.Locked` by the Windows rule: `Locked` is set unless its stored text is "0", "False" or "" (`settings_reader.py:68-73`; an Access Yes = -1 is locked); the first status row whose UID text equals the bid's status wins (`cover_sheet.py:15-23`); duplicate `JobStatuses` UIDs (`settings_reader.py:65`, `require_unique_master_data_uids` inside `_parse_job_statuses` :63-77) or any failed read give an empty list (`settings_reader.py:361-370`) = unresolved = locked; empty status = unlocked; a missing bid = locked. The host tests pin NULL, 0, -1, 1, 2, "False", duplicate UIDs, an unknown status uid and a failed read with oracle rows from the real Windows function. Every NEW writer of M1 to M4 re-checks it INSIDE its own transaction (after the ownership checks) and refuses with a typed result. It checks the bid of the write (a superset of the Windows "active bid" rule). **One UI bridge, S0's**: every UI stream reaches the db_path wrapper through `Services/ImportService+BidLock.h/.mm` with `+ (BOOL)isBidLocked:(NSString *)bidUID` (YES when the bid is locked and also YES when the read failed: fail closed; it calls the wrapper with `[ImportService _dbPath]`, 4.4 item 3) and through the helper `- (BOOL)_refuseIfBidLocked`, declared in S0's marker block `M1 bid lock (S0)` of `ViewController_Internal.h` (6.1) and implemented in S0's `ViewController+BidLock.mm`, which asks the bridge about `self.bidUID`, shows "This bid is locked." (N-M1-02) on the topmost presented controller (the Conditions and Layers sheets are presented over the main one) and answers YES. S1 to S4 call that helper at the top of every blocked action (the page menu items, 4.8; "+" and "Folders" actions of the Conditions sheet; Layers "+"); code that is not a `ViewController` category, the folder manager of 5.2, reaches it through its delegate; no stream adds a lock method of its own. (The research proposed `is_bid_locked(..., bool* resolved)` for New Condition and `bid_is_locked(..., bool&)` for folders: the second signature is the one kept.)
- **Scope**: only the new writes; existing takeoff / condition edits are not retrofitted in M1 (N-M1-01). **UI**: controls stay visible; a tap on a blocked action shows "This bid is locked." through that one helper (N-M1-02), Windows refuses silently (`project_write_service.py:701-704`).
- Reused by: all M1 writers, M2 paste / hole insert, M4 annotations, image modes, overlay writes (their UI calls the same bridge and helper).
- **As built (S0, M1):** the design stands as written: `ost_domain::resolve_bid_status_lock` (`Core/Domain/BidLock.*`), `ost_store::integrity::bid_is_locked` inside each writer's transaction and the `db_path` wrapper `ost_store::bid_is_locked` for the UI (`ProjectStore+BidLock.cpp`, `ProjectStore_BidLock.hpp`), the one UI bridge `+[ImportService isBidLocked:]` and `-[ViewController _refuseIfBidLocked]` (alert titled "Bid Locked", text "This bid is locked."). All six M1 writers re-check the lock inside their transaction after the ownership checks. The New Condition and Delete Page writers refuse a locked bid with an empty error text (the Set Scale bridge has a `locked` out parameter), so the screens ask `_refuseIfBidLocked` before they act, and New Condition also asks `isBidLocked:` when its writer refuses with no text. Reading `JobStatuses` follows the Windows text serialisation: a cell (any column of any row, the Name too) whose text is not valid UTF-8 empties the whole status list, so every bid that has a status then reads as locked (fail closed; open point 9a.7).

### 4.2 UID limit and the 64-bit allocator (M1, step 0)

- Windows UID columns are Access `COUNTER` / Long (32 bit): `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/database_creator.py:157` (condition UID), `:467`, `:474` (takeoff UID, ParentUID). Only `RefNo` is guarded at 2147483647 (`ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/condition_operations.py:56-62`, text `Cannot add N condition(s): the new condition numbers would reach X, past 2147483647, the highest condition number.`). The UID allocator `identity_allocation.py:5-47` (`MAX(UID)+1`, raised above every referencing column) has no ceiling.
- iOS: `next_uid_preserving_references` (`ProjectStore+Integrity.cpp:259-308`) stops only at the 63-bit limit (`parse_uid` refuses values beyond 63 bits: `ProjectStore_Integrity.hpp:18-20`).
- **Chosen design (one)**: a thin wrapper in `ProjectStore_Integrity` (S0): `next_uid_within_access_limit(db, table, count, first_out)` = `next_uid_preserving_references` plus a refusal BEFORE any write when `first + count - 1 > 2147483647`, arithmetic in 64 bit. Every new writer that allocates a UID for a Windows Long column uses it (conditions, folders, layers, takeoffs of paste / hole insert) and `duplicate_condition` is switched to it too (N-M1-04; Windows file:line in register NC-9, LY-7, BO-11, CB-9). Message shape (owner may reword, N-M1-03): `Cannot add a <noun>: no identity is left below 2147483647.`; condition numbers keep the Windows text through `ost_domain::refuse_new_condition_numbers`. (As built: `next_uid_within_access_limit(db, table, count, first_out, bool* no_identity_left = nullptr)` and `no_identity_left_text(noun)` in `ProjectStore_Integrity.hpp`; the optional flag tells the limit refusal from any other failure, so the writers give the text only for the limit. Used by `insert_condition`, `allocate_condition_identities`, the folder and layer writers and `duplicate_condition`.)
- `allocate_condition_identities(count)` (internal, declared in `ProjectStore_ConditionCreate.hpp`, for `count > 1`) is written in M1 (New Condition) so M2 cross-bid paste reuses it; its `count > 1` cases are tested in M1 (5.1).
- Note: the comment at `ProjectStore.hpp:248-255` ("This is the only place that allocates condition numbers") and the sentence "The only iOS allocator is ost_store::duplicate_condition (count 1)" in `ConditionFormRules.hpp:105-118` become stale with New Condition. S0 rewrites both in S0.d (wording: "condition numbers are allocated by insert_condition and duplicate_condition"); S1 does not edit `ProjectStore.hpp`. (As built: both rewritten with that wording; the same stale sentence in a comment of `Config/Tests/Conditions/test_condition_duplicate.cpp` was corrected with the M1 docs, text only. The older docs carry the corrected wording "until M1 the only iOS allocator was duplicate_condition": `PARITY_MAP.md`, `PORTING_BLOCKERS.md`, `PORTING_LOG.md`, `PORTING_STATUS.md` and `.json`, `PARITY_DIVERGENCES.json`.)
- Text limits (N-M1-13, S0.b): the name limits count UTF-16 units and no Core function counted them before. ONE helper pair serves every writer, `ost_domain::utf16_length` and `ost_domain::has_control_character` in `Core/Domain/TextLimits.hpp/.cpp` (6.2 S0.b): S1 (condition name 255, folder name 50), S2 (layer name 50) and S3 (page name 255) call it and none writes its own copy, because inline functions with one name and different bodies in `ost_domain` would be a silent ODR violation.

### 4.3 Popover anchors (iPad) (M1, step 0)

- The seven action sheets of the Edit Condition pickers have no `popoverPresentationController` (`EditConditionViewController+Pickers.mm:54-287`, the seven sheets at :58, :81, :116, :151, :175, :228, :269; grep: zero anchors in `Controllers/EditConditionViewController/`); on iPad an action sheet without an anchor throws. (As built: S0, commit `54ff58e`, added a `popoverPresentationController` anchor to each of the seven pickers; the iPad behaviour is device check M1-01, not run.) The Conditions list, project browser, export and takeoff context menu are anchored (`Controllers/ConditionsViewController.mm:440-446`, `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+Export.mm:36-37`, `Controllers/DatabaseViewController.mm:805-807`).
- **Fix (S0, own commit, own device check)**: anchor each picker on the tapped table row. **Rule for all new work**: every `UIAlertControllerStyleActionSheet` and every `UIPopoverPresentationController` gets `sourceView`/`sourceRect` or `barButtonItem`; prefer `UIAlertControllerStyleAlert` with a text field for names and `UIMenu` for long-press menus (system-anchored). The M1 verification path greps the touched files for `UIAlertControllerStyleActionSheet` and checks each has an anchor. (iPad layout of the M1 screens: N-M1-25.) Reused by every later sheet and popover (M2 snap settings, M3 option sheets, M4 tool and Image menus).

### 4.4 The write pattern (every writer, M1 to M4)

1. One function in `ost_store`, in its own `Components/ProjectStore+<Feature>.cpp` with its declarations in its own header `ProjectStore_<Feature>.hpp` (the typed preferences of 4.13 are the one exception: they share `ProjectStore+Settings.cpp` / `ProjectStore_Settings.hpp`; one `#include` line each in `ProjectStore.hpp`, added together with an empty stub of each header (declarations only: a namespace and the include guard) in step 0 of the milestone that builds the feature (M1: the six headers of S0.d, 6.2, which S0 hands to their streams; M2: one commit before the streams start, 6.5; M3 and M4: when the milestone starts), so parallel features do not edit the same lines and the includes compile from the first commit). Signature style = `duplicate_condition`: `(db_path, bid_uid, ..., std::string* error = nullptr)`; returns the new uid / bool; `error` is set only for texts the user must see.
2. One transaction (`OstDatabase::begin_transaction`, `commit`, `rollback`: `Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.hpp:116-125`; the read-only `read_raw_bid` of M3 uses `begin_read_transaction`, 5.11); inside it, in this order: parse uids (`integrity::parse_uid`), bid exists, rows belong to the OPEN bid (`integrity::row_belongs_to_bid`; Windows checks only "one bid", register LY-9, SS-12, RP-7, DP-6), bid lock (4.1), name / value rules, UID limit (4.2), the writes, `commit`. ANY failed statement = `rollback` and a negative result; nothing throws across the Objective-C boundary.
3. Thin bridge `Services/ImportService+<Feature>.h/.mm` (one category per feature, so `ImportService.h/.mm` is not a merge point) that converts types and calls the store. A category file cannot call the private `+[ImportService _dbPath]` (declared in no header: an undeclared class method is a compile error under ARC) nor the file-static `OSTString` of `ImportService.mm`, so the shared header `Services/ImportService_Internal.h` carries both: the inline helper `OSTString(const std::string&)` (moved out of `ImportService.mm`) and the category declaration `@interface ImportService (OSTInternal)` with `+ (std::string)_dbPath;` (still implemented in `ImportService.mm`). The file is S0's (6.1, 6.2 S0.d); it already exists in the working tree (created after this plan was first written) and is committed as part of S0. Every `Services/ImportService+<Feature>.mm` (and every M3 `ExportService` method or category that reads or writes the store) imports `"ImportService_Internal.h"` and calls `[ImportService _dbPath]` and `OSTString`; `ImportService.h` is not edited and `ImportService.mm` changes only by the removal of the static helper and the one `#import` (the `ImportService.mm` line numbers cited in this plan are those of the baseline `06d8d69`; that edit lowers them by 17).
4. The UI calls the bridge, then applies the refresh listed in 4.11, then shows a result. Pure checks (name rules, plans, confirmation texts) live in `Core/Domain` and are ALSO re-checked inside the store function (the store never trusts the screen; the one exception is the layer in-use refusal: `delete_layers` keeps the Windows contract of clearing the references and deleting, and the refusal is the usage read plus the delete plan, 5.3).

### 4.5 Text versus blob storage

iOS stores memo / long-binary columns (Notes, Description, legend XML, text annotations) as TEXT; the loader reads both (`OstDatabase` has no blob binding: `OstDatabase.hpp:34-38`). Windows writes some of them as BLOB UTF-8. **Decision (engineering): TEXT in M1 and M2, as `update_condition` does today** (an engineering decision; there is no row for it in section 7). M3 reads TEXT or BLOB and writes the Windows forms; the M3 round-trip tests assert this for every column M1 / M2 write (Notes, folder Description NULL, legend XML after Set Scale).

### 4.6 Number formatting parity with Python

Several writers must produce Python's text: `%.3f` trimmed (takeoff positions), exact `repr` (annotation positions: `PositionParser` already has `python_float_repr`), `%.6f` (overlay rect), `{:g}` (Set Scale fields), `%.15g` / `%.15f` trimmed (OST cells, scale labels), `str(int(round(v)))` half-even (CSV), no-exponent PDF numbers. **One home**: `Core/Domain/NumberText.{hpp,cpp}` (has `round_half_even`, `integer_digits`, `format_double`); the missing formatters are added by Set Scale (M1, the first user) with oracle strings generated from Python and checked on MSVC and AppleClang; M3 reuses them. No locale-dependent formatting anywhere.

### 4.7 The undo model

| Action | Windows | iOS today | Plan |
|---|---|---|---|
| Takeoff place / move / delete / property edit / paste | undoable (history targets are takeoffs and annotations only: `ost_visualizer/presentation/services/undo_redo_service.py:25-48`) | Move, MoveGroup, Place, Delete, SetNegative (`UndoRedoController.mm:7`) | M2 adds Paste (and a hole insert reuses Place) |
| New Condition, folders, layers, rename page | no entry; history not cleared | no entry | none (N-M1-06) |
| Set Scale | no entry, but earlier entries rescale on replay via captured page scales (`ost_visualizer/presentation/handlers/plan_view_action_handler.py:1667-1828`) | entries do NOT capture the page scale (replay would write old-unit positions into a rescaled page: data corruption) | **M1 (Set Scale) adds a page-scale field to the file-local `UndoEntry` (`UndoRedoController.mm:9-20`) that only stores the page uid and the pair captured per takeoff of an entry (read through TWO new `@optional` `UndoRedoDelegate` methods, one for the page uid and pair of a takeoff when an entry is created and one for the current pair of a page uid at replay, implemented in S4's `ViewController+PageScale.mm`, 5.4); the replay arithmetic is the Core function `position_rescale_factor_between_page_scales` (4.10) applied through a Core helper `rescale_entry_positions(positions, captured, current)` in `Core/Domain/PageScaleTransform.*`, host-tested in the Settings area; `UndoHistory.hpp` is not edited**; M2 Paste and M4 reuse it; fallback if the owner prefers: clear the history after a scale change (N-M1-06) (as built: the two `@optional` methods are `undoRedoPageScaleForTakeoff:pageUID:` and `undoRedoPageScaleForPage:` (it answers a `std::optional<ost_domain::PageScale>`), implemented in `ViewController+PageScale.mm`, and `ViewController.mm` is not edited for them; the Core pieces are `rescale_entry_positions` in `PageScaleTransform.*` and `page_scale_of_takeoff` / `replay_entry_positions` in `PageScaleCapture.*`; nothing captured replays with factor 1 and a page that is gone at replay refuses the undo / redo, both pinned by tests with the suffix `_DeliberateDifferenceFromWindows`; the controller and delegate path has no host test, device check M1-17) |
| Delete page | entries left stale, refused later (register DP-8) | `_loadProject` clears history (`ViewController.mm:1388-1407`) | clear on success (N-M1-06) |
| Annotations | typed entries, one per gesture | none | M4: one entry per gesture, same history |

### 4.8 The page action menu (built in M1, shared by rename / delete / Set Scale, extended in M4)

ONE entry point (the research proposed a "Page" toolbar button for Set Scale and a long-press for rename / delete; the long-press is the one kept, no new permanent toolbar button in M1 to M3): a long-press on a page button of the page bars (`Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+PageSelector.mm:42-192`) opens a `UIMenu` through one builder, `ViewController+PageMenu.mm`, with the items **Set Scale...**, **Rename Page...**, **Delete Page** (destructive, disabled when the bid has one page) for the 2D bar, and **Rename Page...** only for the 3D bar (N-M1-05). It acts on the PRESSED page (stored raw name, not the label). It needs no popover anchor (system-anchored context menu). Disabled while an area placement is in progress (the callback `planView:areaPlacementInProgress:`, `ViewController.mm:719-737`, touches only the FAB, the snap toggle, the place-undo button (`_placeActionButton`) and the toolbar buttons, never the page bars, and keeps no flag: S3 adds one line there that sets a `BOOL` property of the class extension `@interface ViewController ()`, which S0 declares, 5.5 and 6.1; `ViewController+PageMenu.mm` reads it when the menu is SHOWN) and on a locked bid each item first calls `_refuseIfBidLocked` (4.1), which explains. S3 creates `ViewController+PageMenu.mm` with the Rename item (5.5) and adds Delete (5.6) after it; S4 inserts Set Scale (5.4) as the FIRST item, only after S3's rename has landed (fixed order Set Scale, Rename, Delete; 6.1, 6.2), so no two streams create the same file. The long-press competes with the whole-view 3D long-press recogniser (`ViewController.mm:255-260`), so S3 also adds a guard in `_handle3DLongPress:` (5.5, 6.1); device check M1-14. S3 declares in its marker block `M1 pages A (S3)` of `ViewController_Internal.h` every method of its three page-menu files that another file calls or names in `@selector(...)`, and S4 declares its Set Scale presenter in `M1 pages B (S4)` (6.1). (As built: the menu is a `UIButton.menu` with `showsMenuAsPrimaryAction = NO` whose only child is a `UIDeferredMenuElement` with an uncached provider, so the items are rebuilt each time the menu is shown; the item list and order live in `-[ViewController _pageMenuItemsForPageUID:onBar2D:]` (`ViewController+PageMenu.mm`) and the enabled states in the Core `ost_data::page_menu_state` (`Core/Data/PageMenuRules.*`), which now has the entry `set_scale` (2D bar only; enabled = a bid is open, the page is a page of the open project, no area placement). S4 did not add the Set Scale item: the integrator inserted it as the FIRST item in commit `3367239`, calling `_presentSetScaleForPageUID:` for the PRESSED page, with `_setScaleSubtitleForPageUID:` as the subtitle (the current scale label). Each item first asks `_refuseIfBidLocked`. A long press on a page button of either bar in 3D mode is guarded in `_handle3DLongPress:`; whether the page menu still wins against the whole-view recogniser is device check M1-14, with the fallback in 9a.7.)

### 4.9 Toolbar budget (touch layout)

The bottom toolbar has 7 equal-width buttons: Reset (3D only), Undo, Redo, Delete (2D only), Conds, Layers, Export (`ViewController.mm:303-322`). 2D shows 6, 3D shows 4. **Rule: M1 to M3 add no permanent button.** M1 to M3 entries are long-press items (page menu 4.8, takeoff sheet), the "+" and "Folders" buttons inside the Conditions sheet, the "+" inside the Layers sheet, items in the Export sheet (M3), and contextual chips (backout, snap settings: one chip row above the floating mode button, 5.8 / 5.9). M4 adds ONE 2D button, **Image** (one menu for rotate / flip, Invert, Bitonal, Show mode and the overlay actions: the image-mode and overlay research each proposed their own control; they are merged into one, like the Windows Image menu) and two 3D buttons, **Edges** and **Cuts**. Annotation tools use the floating mode button (`Controllers/ViewController/Components/FloatingModeButton.mm`), not a toolbar slot.

### 4.10 Shared page-scale transform and ratio rule (built in M1 by Set Scale)

Windows has three ratio definitions (`ost_visualizer/domain/services/page_scale_transform.py:9-20`, `domain/entities/overlay.py:7-19`, `application/services/page_visualization_metadata_service.py:20-22`; register SS-4). **Two Core functions, one per Windows contract** (the two differ on purpose): `ost_domain::units_per_sheet_inch(sf1, sf2)` = `overlay.py:7-19` (strict: nullopt unless `sf1` finite and > 0 and the ratio finite and > 0), used by the Set Scale writer, which refuses bad stored scales (`page_operations.py:74-77`), and by the overlay rect; and `ost_domain::position_rescale_factor_between_page_scales(source, target)` = a port of `page_scale_transform.py:9-30` (lenient: `sf1 == 0` -> ratio 1.0, source ratio 0 -> factor 1.0, so the pinned `test_page_scale_transform.py` oracle (0,12)->(0.25,12) = 48 ports unchanged), used by undo replay (M1) and paste redo (M2), whose caller refuses a factor that is not finite and > 0 (register CB-8). Annotation rescale and overlay rect rescale (M4) name in 5.13 / 5.15 which of the two they use. The existing 3D rule `page_scale_ratio` (`ProjectData.hpp:79-87`, tested in `test_page_scale_ratio.cpp`) is left as it is.

### 4.11 What refreshes after each write (one table, so features agree)

| Write | Memory | View refresh | Selection / zoom | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| New Condition | reload conditions of the bid from the DB (`loadProject`-style, conditions only) | Conditions list reload, quantities | new row selected and revealed, no placement | mirrors Duplicate (`ViewController+Conditions.mm:119-270`) |
| Folders (create / rename / delete / move condition) | reload folders + conditions | Conditions list only; no mesh refresh, no undo clear (Windows: `application/condition_change_impact.py:27-82`) | unchanged | |
| Layers (create / rename / move / delete) | `reload_layers` (layers only: `ProjectData::layers` and `merged_layers`, nothing else), rebuild `sortedLayerUIDs` | Layers sheet; conditions' layer names; a rename that touches image / annotation / comments also reloads PlanView and the meshes (`layer_rename_preserves_rendering`, 5.3) | new row selected / scrolled | delete = `reload_layers` as well (an in-use layer is refused, so no condition changes; never `_loadProject:`: the undo history is kept, 4.7) |
| Rename page | `ProjectData::apply_page_name` in place | the page bars, rebuilt with `_populatePageSelector` followed by `_highlightModeLabels` (5.5), + `PageEntry` names only (`refreshPageNames`) | selection, zoom, meshes untouched (Windows: labels only, `ui_event_coordinator.py:3318-3359`) | do NOT use `loadProject:` (resets the selection) |
| Delete page | reload the bid from the DB | `_reloadProjectSelectingPage:` (5.6: `loadProject:selectingPage:`, then the page bars rebuilt with `_populatePageSelector` followed by `_highlightModeLabels`) with the page that followed the deleted one | only that page selected | undo history cleared |
| Set Scale | update `ProjectData` through the memory twin `apply_page_scales` (5.4; as built the free function `ost_domain::apply_page_scales` in `PageScaleTransform.hpp`, not a `ProjectData` member); it must equal a reload of the page(s) from the DB (agreement test), so the success path does not reload | PlanView `loadPage` without resetting zoom only when the active 2D page is among the changed pages, regenerate meshes when a page selected in 3D changed, refresh quantities when the active or a selected page changed (Windows `ui_event_coordinator.py:3318-3359`) | zoom kept | PlanView caches the scale at `loadPage` |

### 4.12 Test infrastructure (what exists, what M1 adds)

- Each of Browser, Conditions, Persistence and Settings has a `TempDb` in `Config/Tests/<Area>/<Area>TestSupport.hpp`; Import has the equivalent `ScratchDb`. Failure injection is `block(table, event)` (a `RAISE(ABORT)` trigger) with `unblock`: in `Persistence/PersistenceTestSupport.hpp:134-142` and `Conditions/ConditionsTestSupport.hpp:205-213`; `Import/ImportTestSupport.hpp:155` has `ScratchDb::block` without `unblock`; `snapshot_all` only in Conditions (`:185`); `seed_two_bids` in `Persistence/StoreTestSupport.hpp:53`. (The research notes called the helper `fail_trigger`; that name does not exist.)
- Placement of the new M1 tests, so no new area is needed and the README table is edited once: Conditions area = New Condition, folders; Persistence area = lock guard, layers, page store functions; Formatting / Parsers / Geometry = Set Scale rules, labels, transforms; Settings = undo capture. M2 to M4 add areas only where the README convention says so (`Config/Tests/<Area>/area.cmake` optional).
- What M1 adds (S0 owns these files, 6.1; the others only read them): in `Config/Tests/Persistence/PersistenceTestSupport.hpp` a port of `snapshot_all` (`ConditionsTestSupport.hpp:185`; `PersistenceTestSupport.hpp` has only `snapshot(tables)` at `:124`) and in `StoreTestSupport.hpp` the seed helpers `seed_locked_bid` and `seed_pages` (it has only `seed_two_bids`, `:53`); S0 adds the limit cases to the existing `test_uid_allocation.cpp`. Each area has its OWN `TempDb` class (`ost_ptest::TempDb` in Persistence, `ost_ctest::TempDb` in Conditions, `ConditionsTestSupport.hpp:33`, `ost_stest::TempDb` in Settings, `ost_btest::TempDb` in Browser), so the helpers above, which take an `ost_ptest::TempDb`, serve only the Persistence-area tests; S1's lock cases of 5.1 and 5.2 (Conditions area) use a `seed_locked_bid` that S1 adds to `Config/Tests/Conditions/ConditionsTestSupport.hpp` (S1 owns it, 6.1; `snapshot_all` already exists there). A stream that needs another helper puts it in its own `<Feature>TestSupport.hpp` in the directory of the area its tests live in (Persistence, Conditions, ...).
- Oracle tables (`Config/Tests/Fixtures/*.txt`, `*OracleData.hpp`) are generated from the Windows tree READ-ONLY with Python (running the real Windows function where it is callable); every oracle row records its Windows file:line. Whole-database snapshots (`snapshot_all`) prove "nothing else changed" and "a refused write leaves the database untouched".

### 4.13 Settings store, share plumbing and shared text helpers

- **Settings** (M2 snap is the first user; M3 and M4 reuse): per-device preferences in the existing `_AppPreferences` table through `get_pref` / `set_pref` (`ProjectStore_Internal.hpp:114-153`), typed wrappers per feature in Core (`SnapPreferences` in 5.9, `SummaryCsvOptions` in 5.10, `PdfExportOptions` in 5.12, `ViewOptions` in 5.17), read and written by typed functions that all live in ONE pair of files, `ProjectStore+Settings.cpp` / `ProjectStore_Settings.hpp` (created in M2 by 5.9; M3 and M4 add their functions there, no other store header for settings), each field validated on its own (a bad stored value = the default of that field only; Windows resets the WHOLE config on one bad value: register SN-7, E-9). The research proposed `NSUserDefaults` for the CSV options and `_AppPreferences` for snap and edges: `_AppPreferences` is the one kept because it is host-testable. `_AppPreferences` is not a Windows table and is never exported (M3).
- **Share plumbing** (M3, Summary CSV builds it): `ExportService` writes the finished file to the temp folder and `UIActivityViewController` shares it with the popover anchor of `ViewController+Export.mm:93-94` (`:36-37` is the export action sheet); background queue, progress, Cancel that removes the temp file, temp cleanup after the share and at launch. OST, OSP and PDF reuse it.
- **Text helpers** (M3, Summary CSV builds them): `strip_elevation_suffix` and `elevation_name_collisions` next to `parse_elevation` in `Core/Domain/PositionParser.*`; the generated HTML-entity table lives in `Core/Import/HtmlUnescape.{hpp,cpp}` + `HtmlEntityData.hpp` (5.10); `reassemble_elevation` (M1, New Condition) goes into `PositionParser.*` too and replaces the Objective-C copy (5.1).

### 4.14 New iOS-only texts

Texts that Windows does not have (owner may reword, N-M1-03): lock `This bid is locked.`; limits (N-M1-13); `Cannot add a <noun>: no identity is left below 2147483647.`; folder delete line `Folders inside it move to the top level.`; browser failure alerts with the existing DB-locked hint (`Core/Domain/BrowserRules.hpp:75-93`, `DB_LOCKED_HINT`; since job 11 the layer failures have no hint, N-M1-17); the Delete Page confirmation texts (5.6; in the Windows style since parity pass 2). Windows texts are kept verbatim wherever Windows has one (listed in each feature section). Since job 11 (owner decision EXP-21, 2026-11-28) the import and export alert titles and texts are NOT iOS-only wording: they are the Windows wording (4.15); "Nothing to Export" no longer exists.

### 4.15 Export and import wording (owner decision EXP-21, 2026-11-28)

The owner decided that every alert of an export or import uses the CURRENT Windows wording (Windows HEAD `d1c430a`: `ost_visualizer/shared/texts.py`, `presentation/texts.py`, `import_handler.py`, `export_service.py`, `presentation/utils/messagebox.py`). It replaces the owner decision of 2026-11-19 (keep the iOS texts), which was based on the old Windows text "Export function returned False." Job 11 applied it to every existing iOS alert (9a.11); this section is the rule for M3 (5.10 CSV, 5.11 OST / OSP, 5.12 PDF), from the first line of code:

1. **One user sentence in the alert; the technical detail goes to the log only** (`NSLog`; Windows `presentation/utils/messagebox.py:130-170` logs the shown text plus the exception and shows the message only). Never show an exception text, errno, SQLite text, parser reason or writer error, never write "Error creating <fmt> export:" or "Failed to create ... export:", never ask the user to check the log. On iOS the export paths log the technical reason with `NSLog` (review fix 9856e4c, `Services/ExportService.mm`): nothing to export, a file name that is not valid UTF-8, and an OBJ, DXF or HTML write failure (with the path and `errno`); the import paths log the raw diagnostic (`ImportService.mm`). Full sentences end with a period; "Failed to ..." for errors; US spelling.
2. **Typed result, text in Core.** The sentences live in Core and have host tests that pin them byte for byte; the Objective-C only calls them. Built in job 11: `Core/Domain/FailureText.hpp/.cpp` with `ExportFailureKind` (`NoFileWritten`, `Unexpected`, `WriteFailed`) and `export_failure_text(kind, cause)` (title "Export Error", NO `format_name` parameter: the OBJ / DXF / HTML sentences are the format-less ones), `ImportFailureKind` with `classify_import_failure(raw)` and `import_failure_text(kind, format, context, file_name)`, `load_failure_reason(target)` and `folder_delete_alert(reason)`; the raw import diagnostics are shared constants in `Core/Import/ImportDiagnostics.hpp` (the producers and the classifier use the same text). `Config/Tests/Browser/test_failure_text.cpp` pins it. Not wired yet, for M3: the vocabulary `FileFailureCause` (`NotFound`, `Denied`, `DiskFull`), `file_failure_reason`, `file_write_failure_reason`, `cause_from_errno` (ENOENT, EACCES / EPERM, ENOSPC) and `write_failure_text(cause)`: the iOS writers (`obj_writer.cpp`, `dxf_writer.cpp`, `threejs_writer.cpp`) return a bool and the store functions return text only, so no known cause can reach the alert today. The M3 writers return a typed result like Windows `ExportErrorCode` (`NoData`, `WorkerFailed`, `WriteFailed(cause)`, `Unexpected`) and record `errno` right after the failed open / write. The handler-level sentence with "of the {format_name} file" (item 4) and its `format_name` parameter are added to `FailureText` WITH M3 (the first writer that needs it), not before.
3. **Titles:** failures "Export Error" (`presentation/texts.py:801`), no data "No Data" (`:819`), success "Export Complete" (`:818`; iOS may keep the share sheet without a success alert), PDF without a valid page "No Valid Pages" (`:809`). The iOS title "Nothing to Export" does not exist any more.
4. **Sentences (exact, Windows HEAD):**
   - No data, pages: "No takeoffs found for any of the selected pages." (`shared/texts.py:291-293`; already the iOS text); CSV: "No summary rows are available to export." (`shared/texts.py:757-759`, `summary_csv_export_service.py:83-100`).
   - Nothing written (the strategy returned False; built as `NoFileWritten`): "No file was written. Check that the selected pages have takeoffs this format can export, then try again or choose a different destination." (`shared/texts.py:274-277`, `export_service.py:157-173`).
   - Unexpected (service level; OBJ / DXF / HTML / CSV / OST / OSP / PDF writers; built as `Unexpected`): "An unexpected error stopped the export. Please try again or choose a different destination; if the problem continues, contact support." (`shared/texts.py:278-281`, `export_service.py:182-189`, `summary_csv_export_service.py:125-133`, `osp_exporter.py:176-183`, `pdf_exporter.py:283-290`, `ost_exporter.py:713-728`).
   - Unexpected (handler level, or no message at all; M3): "An unexpected error stopped the export of the {format_name} file. Please try again or choose a different destination; if the problem continues, contact support." with format names "OST", "OSP", "PDF", "Summary CSV" (`presentation/texts.py:830-833`, `export_handler.py:340-349,548-557,559-587`). This is the only place the "of the <fmt> file" variant is used; for OBJ / DXF / HTML Windows uses the format-less sentence (a correction of the parity pass 2 rows A5-15 and A6-08).
   - Write failure, generic (built as `WriteFailed`): "The file could not be written. Please ensure you have write permissions to the destination folder and try again." (`shared/texts.py:282-285`; on iOS there is no destination folder dialog, the file goes to the temp folder and the share sheet: the owner said adopt the Windows wording as it is). With a known cause (M3): "The file could not be written. {reason}" (`shared/texts.py:286-288`, `file_error_reasons.py:47-64`); a missing file becomes the destination-folder sentence. Reasons used on iOS (`shared/texts.py:57-75`): "The destination folder was not found. Choose another folder and try again." / "Access to the file was denied. Check that you have permission to use the file and its folder." / "There is not enough space on the disk. Free up space and try again." ("The file is open in another program. Close it there and try again." and "The network location is not available. Check the network connection and try again." have no iOS case.) The OSP and PDF write failures, the CSV write failure and the PDF "merge pages" failure use the same two sentences (`osp_exporter.py:165-175`, `pdf_exporter.py:247-282`, `summary_csv_export_service.py:113-124`); a native PDF writer error is never shown.
   - OST with invalid references: "Cannot export OST because some data in this bid has invalid references. Please contact support." (`shared/texts.py:1868-1871`, `ost_exporter.py:640-647`).
   - OSP with missing drawing files: "Cannot export OSP because referenced drawing files are missing: {preview}" and, with more than 10: "Cannot export OSP because referenced drawing files are missing: {preview}; and {more_count} more." (`presentation/texts.py:2338-2344`, `osp_exporter.py:85-101`; {preview} = the first 10 names joined with "; ").
   - PDF, page whose drawing is gone or empty: "The drawing file for page {page_name} is missing or no longer has that page. Check the page's drawing file, then try again." and "The drawing for page {page_name} has no visible page area. Check the page's drawing file, then try again." (`presentation/texts.py:2347-2354`, `pdf_exporter.py:600-625`); PDF with no valid page: "No valid pages selected for export." (`presentation/texts.py:810`); PDF over its own source: title "Invalid Save Location", "Cannot save over a selected base or overlay source file.\nPlease choose a different filename or location." (`presentation/texts.py:813-816`).
   - Stale context: title "Export Cancelled": "The selected bid or page changed while the save dialog was open. Please start the export again." (`presentation/texts.py:790-793`) and "A selected page changed before the save dialog opened. Please start the export again." (`:805-808`).
5. **Progress frame:** one frame "Exporting: <step>..." (`PROGRESS_DIALOG_LABEL_EXPORTING`, `presentation/texts.py:1149`; the step is the name: "Writing OST" `shared/texts.py:1872`; OSP steps "Building OST", "Writing metadata", "Collecting images", "Packaging archive" and "Collecting {file_name}" `presentation/texts.py:2333-2337`; PDF: the page name, or "Page {n}" for an unnamed page, `pdf_exporter.py:172-176`). Import: "Importing: <file name>..." (`:1154`).
6. **Success text (if a confirmation is shown):** "Successfully exported {page_count} page to {filename}" / "... {page_count} pages to {filename}" (`presentation/texts.py:824-829`), PDF one page "Successfully exported page to {filename}" (`:1702-1704`), bid "Successfully exported bid to {filename}{elevation_collision_note}" (`:1432-1434`), summary "Successfully exported Summary to {filename}{elevation_collision_note}" (`:1423-1425`).
7. **Elevation-collision note** (CSV with "drop elevation" and OST / OSP with the drop option; appended to the success text): for one name: "\n\nNote: after dropping elevations, 1 condition name became identical ({shown}). Each condition was still exported as its own entry; none were merged."; for N: "\n\nNote: after dropping elevations, {collision_count} condition names became identical ({shown}). Each condition was still exported as its own entry; none were merged." (`presentation/texts.py:780-788`). {shown} = at most 3 entries written `"<name>" (<count>)` joined by ", " (whitespace of the name collapsed), more than 3: "{shown} and {hidden_count} more" (`:789`; `export_handler.py:81-100`).
8. **Import sentences (built in job 11 for the existing import alerts; the M3 round trip must not add others).** Title "Import Error" (`presentation/texts.py:922`). The LOCAL import (the Project browser's Import button) follows `import_handler.py:218-246`: EVERY failure of the file, whatever the cause (not XML, no bid, invalid takeoffs, invalid references, a bad OSP package), shows "The OST file is damaged or uses an unsupported format, so it cannot be imported." (OSP: "The OSP file is damaged ...", `presentation/texts.py:945-948`); the store, schema and temporary-folder failures are included, because Windows `import_ost` swallows every exception and returns False (`ost_importer.py:94-107`); the sentence "An unexpected error stopped the import of the OST file. Please check that the file is valid and try again; if the problem continues, contact support." (`presentation/texts.py:949-952`) is shown only for a genuinely unexpected condition: a C++ exception caught in `ImportService.mm` (`importOSTFile:` and `importOSPFile:` wrap their bodies in try/catch) or a nil `NSError` after a failed import. The per-case Windows sentences ("The OST file contains no bid, so it cannot be imported." `shared/texts.py:1179-1181`; "Some takeoffs in the OST file are invalid, so it cannot be imported." `:1182-1184`; "The imported project contains invalid references." `:1176-1178`) reach a Windows dialog only on the SQL import, so iOS does not show them (the classifier keeps the kinds `NoBid`, `TakeoffGraphInvalid`, `InvalidReferences` for the log; the reason text only goes to `NSLog`). A file handed to the app (Files, Open in, AirDrop; the Windows "args" case, `main_window.py:1198`) shows one line "<file name>: <sentence>" (the file NAME, not the sandbox path) with the format-less sentences of `shared/texts.py:863-885`: "The file is damaged or uses an unsupported format, so it cannot be imported.", "An unexpected error stopped the import. Please try again; if the problem continues, contact support.", "File does not exist.". The other alerts of the same decision: load failure frame "Error Loading File" / "Failed to load <name>:" with the reason "The file could not be read. It may be damaged or may not be an OST database." (project list; `shared/texts.py:1238-1240`) or "An unexpected error stopped opening the bid. Please try again; if the problem continues, contact support." (bid; `shared/texts.py:316-318`); a folder delete that fails reads "Delete Error" / "Failed to delete projects. The database may be locked by another application." and only "Some selected projects still have bids inside." keeps the title "Cannot Delete Project" (`presentation/texts.py:1004-1013`); the page-delete reload failure keeps the iOS sentence and drops the developer reason.

Texts the decision left unchanged on iOS: every sentence that was already the Windows text (browser failures, layers, pages, Set Scale, conditions, "No Data"), and the iOS-only alerts "Not Saved", "Bid Locked", "Failed to delete the condition.", "Failed to save the display settings."
