- NEW UI: `ViewController+PageMenu.mm` (the shared page menu, created by this feature), `ViewController+PageRename.mm`; CHANGED `ViewController+PageSelector.mm` (S3 owns it, 6.1: `_selectorButtonWithTitle:` `:264-280` attaches the long-press menu to the page buttons of the 2D and 3D bars, not to the 3D folder buttons that the same factory makes), `ViewController.mm` (only the area-placement flag line in `planView:areaPlacementInProgress:` and the `_handle3DLongPress:` guard, both described under Touch adaptation; the flag property itself is declared by S0 in the class extension of `ViewController_Internal.h`)
- NEW tests: `Config/Tests/Persistence/test_page_rename_rules.cpp`, `test_page_rename_store.cpp`

**Touch adaptation.** iPhone and iPad: long-press on a page button (2D or 3D bar) -> "Rename Page..." in the shared page menu (4.8); the pressed page is renamed, so the Windows Previous / Next buttons are dropped. The editor is a `UIAlertController` alert (works on iPhone and iPad, no anchor): the old name in the message ("Old name: <name>"), one text field pre-filled and selected, no auto-capitalisation or auto-correct, Cancel / Rename (preferred). Blank -> the Windows "Validation Error" text, then the alert returns with the draft; write failure -> "Save Failed" / "Failed to rename page." then the alert returns with the draft; success -> apply in memory, refresh names, rebuild the bars with `_populatePageSelector` followed by `_highlightModeLabels` (the only existing caller does both, `ViewController.mm:1399-1400`; `_populatePageSelector` ends in `_highlightSelection`, which resets the 2D / 3D chips to the dim colour); no mesh regeneration, no plan reload, no selection change, no undo. Disabled while an area placement is in progress: S3 adds one line to the `planView:areaPlacementInProgress:` callback (`ViewController.mm:719-737`, which today does not touch the page bars) that stores it in a `BOOL` property `areaPlacementInProgress` declared in the class extension `@interface ViewController ()` of `ViewController_Internal.h` (`:26-39`, which `@implementation ViewController` synthesises; S0 adds the property in S0.d next to the marker blocks): a property declared inside a marker block would sit in the category `ViewController (Internal)` (`:42-84`), which has no storage and no accessors, so the setter in the callback would raise 'unrecognized selector'. `ViewController+PageMenu.mm` reads it when the menu is SHOWN (a `UIDeferredMenuElement` with an uncached provider, iOS 15, or a `UIContextMenuInteraction` configuration), never once in `_populateBar:`, so the item states are current. The same S3 edit set adds a guard to `_handle3DLongPress:` (`ViewController.mm:520`) that returns when the touch starts inside `pageBar3D` or `pageBar2D` (for each bar: `!bar.hidden && CGRectContainsPoint(bar.bounds, [lp locationInView:bar])`): both bars are shown in both modes (`_switchToMode2D:` never hides them, `_populatePageSelector` hides them only for a bid without pages), so in 3D mode a long press on a 2D page button reaches this handler too; the whole-view recogniser has no delegate and the camera recognisers already exclude the page bars (`:239-240`), so without the guard a long press on a page button of either bar in 3D mode would pick the takeoff under the finger or select all takeoffs of the selected condition while the page menu opens. The guard is added in M1 without waiting for M1-14, which only confirms it.

**Dependencies.** None for the rule and the store; this feature creates the page menu (`ViewController+PageMenu.mm`, first item Rename Page...); Delete (5.6) and Set Scale (5.4) add their items after it has landed (4.8, 6.1, 6.2). Lock guard; M3 round trip: a name over 255 UTF-16 units or with NUL would break re-opening an iOS export on Windows (N-M1-13).

**Risk.** Core low (one statement, patterns exist). Objective-C medium only for gesture interplay (page-button long press vs the whole-view `_handle3DLongPress:` with no delegate, `ViewController.mm:255-260`) and the page-bar rebuild (scroll offset; a 255-unit name makes one button as wide as its text + 20 because the button width follows the text, `ViewController+PageSelector.mm:159`: default no truncation in M1, judged on device check M1-14 "very long name", N-M1-27). Wrong turns to avoid: `loadProject:` for names (resets the selection), mesh or plan refresh after a rename.

**Size and order of work.** S (estimate: ~50 tests). Order: (1) rule + store tests red; (2) `PageRules` + `rename_page`; (3) in-memory apply; (4) page menu hook + alert; (5) device pack rows.

**Windows oddities and the safe behaviour kept** (register RP-1 to RP-9): RP-1 `rename_page_dialog.py:146-148` - the unchanged check compares the stripped typed text with the UNSTRIPPED stored name, so a stored name with surrounding spaces is rewritten stripped on OK, and a case-only change is a write -> kept and pinned with tests. RP-2 `rename_page_dialog.py:77`, `page_operations.py:130-152`, `database_creator.py:373` - no length limit; > 255 probably escapes as a driver error -> refuse names over 255 UTF-16 code units (the same unit as the 50-unit folder and layer limits of N-M1-13) before any write (N-M1-13). RP-3 `project_write_service.py:6354-6369` - a committed rename is reported as "Failed to rename page." when the reload fails -> report the write result. RP-7 `page_operations.py:135` - the writer has no bid argument -> `bid_uid` checked. RP-8 `mdb_writer.py:298-302` - row count ignored -> exactly one row changed or rollback. RP-4 no live re-sort (`page_combo.py:280-291` vs `hierarchy_reader.py:230-239`) -> kept. RP-6 the Cover Sheet can store an empty page name (`settings_operations.py:352`, `:423`) -> iOS rename refuses empty; an empty stored name still loads and shows its uid. RP-5 `page_combo.py:27-29` vs `ui_event_coordinator.py:4846` - an empty name shows the uid in the selector but the raw empty name in the status panel -> always `page_label`. RP-9 `mdb_writer.py:75-93`; `writer_router.py:117-127` - exceptions other than the missing-owner error escape instead of returning False (inferred) -> every failure is a return value.

**As built (M1).** Core `PageRules.*` (`normalize_page_name`, `decide_page_rename`, the texts, `can_rename_page`) and `ProjectData::apply_page_name`; the UI rules `Core/Data/PageMenuRules.*` and `Core/Domain/PageDialogText.*`; store `rename_page` (`ProjectStore+PageRename.cpp`); bridge `+[ImportService renamePageWithUID:inBid:toName:error:]` (a variant without the error out-parameter is declared and has no caller); `ProjectService refreshPageNames` and `loadProject:selectingPage:`. Differences from the text above: (1) the rule order is blank, then NUL or invalid UTF-8 ("Page name contains a character that cannot be saved."), then over 255 units, then unchanged, so a stored name over 255 units typed again unchanged answers "too long" (pinned, flagged AMBIGUITY in the test; the owner may decide otherwise); (2) the store re-runs the whole name rule and refuses a blank or unstripped name, where the Windows writer accepts any string (RP-1b); (3) a page uid present in two bids is refused for both (`COUNT(*) = 1`, Windows `DuplicateBidOwnedUidError`); (4) the alert shows "Old name: <stored name>" as its message, the field has the placeholder "New name" (the first build had "Old Name:" and "New Name"; parity pass 2 follows the Windows d26893c labels), the name is selected with `selectedTextRange` on the next run-loop turn, Return puts the keyboard away; (5) the bar scroll offsets are restored after the rebuild. The chips of the 2D / 3D bars are repainted by `_highlightModeLabels` after a rename, a delete and a Set Scale mismatch; `_highlightSelection` still dims them on the next page-button tap (pre-existing, 9a.7).

**Tests-first plan (M1).** Pin from source first (no Windows test): blank -> `Refused`, strip, unchanged, case-only, duplicates allowed (`rename_page_dialog.py:141-148`). Port: rename of page 20 only, missing Name column, missing page 99, malformed uids ('abc', '0', '-5', ''), orphan page of a missing bid, the open-guard table (no active page / no bid / denied / blank uid / page not in list -> no dialog), unknown target -> false before any write, labels (`'5 - S9 - Renamed'`). Data safety (new): 255 vs 256 UTF-16 units (including a name whose last character is an astral character that crosses the boundary), NUL, page of another bid, zero rows changed, `TempDb::block` rollback, locked bid writes nothing.

### 5.6 Delete page (M, risk HIGH consequence)

**What Windows does.** Deletes the ACTIVE page from the 2D / 3D context menu only (needs the takeoff tab, page-settings permission, more than 1 page). A page with takeoff, annotation, comment, named view or hot link asks "Delete Verification" (default No); an empty page is deleted without a question. The write is ONE transaction (`settings_operations.py:562-577`, cascade `:698-891`): the bid is derived from the pages (`require_single_bid_scope_for_uids`), then 15 steps in this order (`settings_operations.py:698-891`): 1 clear same-bid `MasterPageUID`; 2 select the pages' comments, detach their replies; 3 select the pages' takeoff uids; 4 clear survivor takeoff self-references (`ParentUID`, `TypGroupTakeoffUID`, `TypPageTakeoffUID`, `TypGroupMarkerUID`); 5 delete percents of those takeoffs; 6 delete dimensions / lines / arrows linked to them by either endpoint; 7 `AffectDPCTypGroupViews`; 8 the 23 `PAGE_DELETE_CHILD_TABLES` by `BidPageUID`; 9 takeoffs; 10 hot links to the pages' named views; 11 hot links; 12 named views; 13 clear `BidSettings.BidPageSelectedUID`; 14 clear `Bids.CoverSheetSelItemUID` (type 1); 15 `BidPages`. Conditions, layers, areas, folders, other pages (no renumbering) and image files are untouched. Afterwards only the page that followed the deleted one in tree order (the previous one if last) is selected; not undoable. The last-page rule ("Cannot delete the last page in the bid.") is UI-only.

**Windows modules.**
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/coordinators/ui_event_coordinator.py:5881-5968`, `:5989-6015`, `:1100-1131`, `:1137-1170` - `can_delete_current_page`, `delete_current_page` (scan, confirm, last-page rule at `:5893`, `:5995-6001`, stage next page, write, error dialogs), `_stage_selection_after_page_delete` (`:5989-6007`, display name `:6009-6015`), `_stage_takeoff_restore` (`:1100-1131`: resets the selected area and the placement condition), `_resolve_takeoff_page_choice` (`:1137-1170`)
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/utils/messagebox.py:8`, `:25-27`, `:52-61` - `DB_LOCKED_HINT`, Yes / No confirm (default No), `confirm_delete_page_with_contents`
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/utils/view_context_menu.py:446-459` - "Rename Page..." and "Delete Page"
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/settings_operations.py:562-577`, `:698-891` - `delete_pages`, `_delete_pages_cascade` (child delete loop `:814-831`)
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/constants.py:4`, `:68-134` - `PAGE_DELETE_CHILD_TABLES` (23), `PAGE_DELETE_CONFIRMATION_TABLES` (15), `COVER_SHEET_PAGE_SELECTION_TYPE=1`
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/database/bid_owned_identity.py:6`, `:105-124`, `:205-235` - chunk 50, uid rules, single-bid scope

**Touch adaptation.** Long-press on a 2D page-bar button -> destructive "Delete Page" in the shared menu (4.8), acting on the pressed page, disabled with one page; no entry on the 3D bar (N-M1-05). A centred `UIAlertController` "Delete Verification" (Cancel default, Delete destructive), ALWAYS shown on iOS (pages cannot be re-added), shorter text for an empty page, accurate wording (no "Alternates / Change Orders", N-M1-23). iPhone and iPad identical (alert needs no anchor). After success: reload from the DB selecting the next page (2D and 3D, only that page; DP-10), undo history cleared, placement mode left, selected takeoff and area cleared, quantities refreshed, no message. A failed content scan (read-failed) refuses the delete before any alert: title `Delete Page`, text `Failed to verify page contents. The database may be locked by another application.` (`ui_event_coordinator.py:5915-5921`). A failed write: title `Delete Page`, text `Failed to delete page. The database may be locked by another application.` (`:5962-5968`; the hint is Windows `DB_LOCKED_HINT`); selection unchanged, nothing written. Confirmation texts (iOS wording, N-M1-03; in the Windows d26893c style since parity pass 2: double-quoted page name, lower-case "page", a full stop, "hotlinks"; Named Views keeps its capitals), title `Delete Verification`: page with content `The page "<name>" contains takeoffs, annotations, comments, Named Views or hotlinks. Deleting this page will remove the page and everything on it from this bid.\n\nDeleting a page cannot be undone. Are you sure you want to delete this page?`; empty page `Delete the page "<name>"?\n\nDeleting a page cannot be undone.`; `<name>` = `page_delete_display_name`: the name, else the last component of `image_path` split on `\` then `/`, else `Page <uid>` (`ui_event_coordinator.py:6009-6015`).

**Dependencies.** Lock guard; the page menu (5.5); no blocking feature dependency. Later: M2 paste onto a deleted page is refused by `insert_takeoff_in` (`ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp:319-321`); M3 and M4 rely on the cascade covering ALL page tables (schema-driven catalog test), including the annotation, named-view and hot-link tables from day one. Set Scale (5.4) rescales 17 position tables (`PAGE_CONTENT_TABLES` + `BidTypGroupViews`, `page_operations.py:26`); all 17 are inside the 26 page tables, so the same catalog test guards both features.

**Risk.** Consequence HIGH (irreversible; iOS cannot add a page back; cascade over 26 tables; orphan rows would later be rejected or adopted by a reused UID). Algorithmic low-medium (simple tested SQL, same table list already proven by the bid cascade). Main risks: table-coverage drift (mitigated by the catalog test), untestable UI state after reload, accidental touch deletion.

**Size and order of work.** M (all figures are estimates: ~300 Core lines, 65-75 tests, ~120 Objective-C++ lines). Order: (1) catalog test + 22 persistence tests red; (2) scan + plan tests red; (3) store + Core; (4) bridge + reload path; (5) menu item + alert; (6) device pack rows.

DP-3 `messagebox.py:55-60` - the text claims removal in "Base Bid and Alternates/Change Orders" and names only some content (cascade is one bid) -> accurate text, Windows trigger set kept (named views and hot links trigger it, legends never). DP-4 `ui_event_coordinator.py:5926-5930` - empty pages are deleted silently -> iOS always confirms. DP-5 `ui_event_coordinator.py:5893`, `:5995-6001` - the last-page rule is UI-only; the writer would delete all pages -> Core refuses. **As built (M1).** Core `PageDeletion.*`, `Core/Data/PageDeletePlan.*`, and, for the screens, `Core/Data/PageMenuRules.*` (`page_menu_state`, `page_delete_has_content`: the scan OR a loaded takeoff, `page_selection_after_reload`, `page_delete_refusal_text`) and `Core/Domain/PageDialogText.*` (`page_delete_store_failure_text`, `page_delete_reload_failed_text`): the decisions that a first version of the Objective-C took (a string compare with `LAST_PAGE_MESSAGE`) moved into Core in commit `699f100`. Store `delete_pages(db_path, bid_uid, page_uids, error*)` and `pages_with_delete_content`; bridge `+[ImportService deletePages:inBid:error:]` (NO with a nil error = failed statement, failed read or locked bid; NO with the last-page text for the last page) and `+pagesWithDeleteContentInBid:error:` (nil when the scan fails: "Failed to verify page contents..." and nothing is deleted). Differences from the text above: (1) steps 13 and 14 (the selected-page and cover-sheet clears) are NOT bid scoped in Windows and are kept so: they clear every bid that names the page, as steps 3 to 6 are bid scoped and step 9 is not (each pinned by a test); (2) `pages_with_delete_content` first probes the file (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sqlite_master`, because SQLite opens a non-database without complaint), a missing `BidPages` table is a success with an empty set, a `BidPages` without `UID` or `BidUID` or with duplicate page uids is a failure; (3) uid texts above 63 bits that Python accepts are refused by `parse_uid`; (4) the page that follows is selected after the DELETED page, even when another page was being viewed (the menu acts on the pressed page: DP-10 kept, flagged for the device pack); (5) the reload also calls `fitCameraToMeshes`, which is not in the call list above; (6) a committed delete whose re-read fails shows an alert that says the page was deleted, and the bid closes when it is dismissed because memory still holds the page (DP-2); (7) `DelPageSlow` waits the real 5 s busy timeout and `DelPageRollback` puts a trigger on each of the 33 statements, so the area runs slower.

**Tests-first plan (M1).** Port 22 persistence tests (cascade rows per table, clears, either-endpoint annotations, duplicate-uid refusals, sparse schema, 50-chunk edges), 8 scan tests (15 tables, legends excluded, only this bid's pages, failure -> read-failed), ~15 pure rules (can-delete, next page a->b, b->c, c->b, display name); the schema-driven catalog test as an iOS test against `BID_RELATIONSHIPS` / `PAGE_DELETE_CHILD_TABLES` so a new schema table fails the build. Data safety (new): `TempDb::block(table, "DELETE")` with a seeded row on the 2nd, 12th and 23rd child table, on `BidTakeoffs`, and on `BidPages` (the last statement), so the selection and cover-sheet clears (steps 13 and 14) are proven rolled back together with the earlier steps (MasterPageUID, comment detach, takeoff self-references, percents); in every case a `snapshot_all`-style comparison shows nothing changed, last page refused, foreign-bid page, mixed bids, empty list refused, lock refusal writes nothing, a bid with a locked job status keeps all rows.

**MILESTONE M2 - placement tools**

### 5.7 Clipboard: copy, paste, duplicate of takeoffs (L, risk medium)

**What Windows does.** The Windows clipboard is an IN-MEMORY object, not the OS clipboard: it holds detached takeoff copies, their raw extras, the source bid, the database path and a snapshot of the source conditions, and it works only inside the same database (on iOS every bid is in the one `ost_projects.sqlite`, `Services/ImportService.mm:36-44`, so the rule is always met and a paste between bids of two different imports is allowed; CB-6 / N-M2-05 apply to it). Copy takes the selected takeoffs plus all descendants of the current page. Paste lands on the CURRENT page (its BidAreaUID is the area selected in the page bar, `plan_view_action_handler.py:5100`; iOS has no area bar, so every pasted row gets BidAreaUID unassigned, like the iOS Place commit, `ViewController.mm:1334`, and the source's `area_uid` is never copied), moves every complete coordinate pair by the snap increment (1.0 when snap is off) or, with Intelligent Paste, moves the first regular takeoff's first point to the pointer. It writes everything in ONE atomic mutation: cross-bid, the conditions are copied first, then takeoffs go in parent-first batches with a source-to-new uid map; extras are written with a fresh GUID and, cross-bid, the six bid-scoped columns are nulled. One history entry follows: undo deletes the created takeoffs, redo replays the payload re-scaled to the current page scale. Duplicate = copy + paste. Cut does NOT exist for takeoffs (disabled).

**New rules of Windows d26893c for the paste drag (commit `df78f67`, to port; the other ten commits of the range do not change M2 behaviour; lines are Windows HEAD `d26893c`, the paths are relative to `ost_visualizer/`).** (1) The pasted anchor: `_prepare_plan_items_paste` now returns the source anchor AND the pasted anchor (source anchor + paste delta, `presentation/handlers/plan_view_action_handler.py:5264-5269`), the handler hands both to `mark_intelligent_paste_drag_pending(pasted_uids, source_anchor, pasted_anchor_ost=...)` (`:4727-4735`; SQL `:5103-5115`; `presentation/components/plan_view/view.py:3684-3707`) and the guided drag starts from the PASTED anchor (`view.py:3739-3742`), never from the order of the pasted uids (before, the anchor was the first uid of the writer's text-sorted map, so mostly one guide pair showed). The pasted anchor is the copy of the very item that gave the source anchor, so both anchors are the same point of the same item and the guide test is exact. Without a pasted anchor the view falls back to the first pending item with the ink header skipped (`presentation/utils/annotation_paste.py:4-12`; annotation, M4). (2) Rotated pages: the guide flags are SCREEN axes (`|dy_screen| <= 8` px = horizontal guide, `|dx_screen| <= 8` px = vertical guide, boundary inclusive, `view.py:3891-3918`, `:218`); the OST correction swaps the flags when the page transform swaps axes, `|m11| < |m12|`, that is rotation 90 or 270 with or without flip (`_snapped_intelligent_paste_delta :3872-3885`, `_page_transform_swaps_axes :3887-3889`); before, the group jumped along the other screen axis while the tested axis' guides showed. (3) Lifecycle of the guided drag: armed only by a committed paste that also selected the pasted items; single use (the first drag consumes it); dropped when the selection no longer holds every pending uid (`view.py:3653-3662`), on Escape (`plan_view/components/input_handler.py:2520-2524`), on a new mark, when the preference is off and when the drag set does not contain the pending set; the SQL Server paste arms it too now (WINDOWS-ONLY). (4) Context-menu Delete: a right-click on the edge or vertex of an already selected area or polygon / cloud annotation keeps the multi-selection (`input_handler.py:3117-3130`, `not in self._selected_uids`; before `!= {target}`), so Delete removes every selected item plus descendants, and an unselected item is selected alone; on iOS this becomes the long-press rule of N-M2-04. The Conditions-sidebar scrolling of the same commit is Windows UI only. (5) CB-12, the snap rule of the group drag, an M2 INPUT, NOT IMPLEMENTED on iOS (Windows `d1c430a`, 2026-11-27, fixed there; first recorded here at `d26893c` as a possible Windows defect, now a rule to port; paths relative to `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/`). With snap on (increment > 0) the axis that the paste guides aligned is NEVER rounded to the snap increment; only an unguided axis snaps. After the axis snap `_snapped_intelligent_paste_delta` (`view.py:3874-3888`) records the guided OST axes (guided x, guided y) = the screen flags after the page-transform swap (swap when |m11| < |m12|); `intelligent_paste_guided_axes` (`view.py:3890-3893`) reports them only while the guided drag is active ((False, False) otherwise; default `drag_handler.py:266-267`, reset `view.py:533`, `:3779`). Case 1, group (every drag of the pasted set, `input_handler.py:1741-1747`, used at `:1810`, `:1855`, `:1892`): per axis delta = the exact aligned delta if guided, else round(delta / inc) * inc (the DELTA is rounded). Case 2, single item or anchor move (handle -1, `drag_handler.py:307-309`; ink: first point, `input_handler.py:1714-1716`; `_snapped_anchor_move`, `drag_handler.py:266-277`): per axis move = the exact delta if guided, else round((anchor + delta) / inc) * inc - anchor (the ABSOLUTE anchor is rounded). Case 3, one-point handle (an item with exactly one point, `drag_handler.py:346-350`): as case 2 and new point = point + move; items with two or more points keep rounding the absolute handle position on both axes. The guide lines are drawn from the same exact preview, so a group dropped on the guides lands exactly on the aligned position. The plan already specified case 1 for iOS (increment 0 to `compute_group_translation_positions` for the guided axis, 5.7 touch adaptation); cases 2 and 3 (single item or anchor, one-point handle) are now part of the rule for the M2 step 3 drag code and for `test_paste_axis_snap.cpp`, with no change of the size estimate. NOTHING of this is built: M2 is NOT started. Numbers (Windows tests, `tests/presentation/components/plan_view/test_view.py`): increment 10, source anchor (10,20), anchor start (51,75), items [51,75,55,79] and [85,100,89,104]: raw (-35,0) -> (-41,0), vertical guides x = 10 and 48 (old 11 and 49); raw (0,-50) -> (0,-55), horizontal guides y = 20 and 49 (old 15 and 44); both axes: horizontal 20 and 49, vertical 10 and 48. Boundary 8 px inclusive. `IntelligentPasteSnapIncrementTests`: increments 1, 7, 12, 25 x screen offsets (0,0), (0,60), (60,0), (3,-4), a horizontal guide -> landed dy = 0, a vertical guide -> landed dx = 0 (6 places); single count ['50'], area + line ['70','40'], mixed ['40','50','70','a1','a3'], rotation 90 / 180 / 270 x flip_x ['40','50']. iOS example data: increment 1.0, source anchor (10,30), pasted start (13.37,42.8), raw delta (-3.2,-12.6): flags (T,T) -> the anchor ends at exactly (10,30) (the old Windows result was (10.37,29.8)); flags (F,F) -> (-3,-13). No host test of iOS pins the single-item and one-point cases yet. Test facts to pin (`tests/integration/placement/test_intelligent_paste_guides.py`): for each of the 9 items of a mixed selection taken as the first copied (areas, hole, attachment, linear, count, negative area, line / text / ink annotations) and dropped on the originals the result is 2 horizontal + 2 vertical guides and a snapped delta of (0,0); the screen offsets (0,0), (0,5), (-6,0), (3,-4), (0,60), (60,0), (40,-40) give 2 horizontal guides if `|oy| <= 8`, 2 vertical guides if `|ox| <= 8`, and the snapped offset (0 if `|ox| <= 8` else ox, 0 if `|oy| <= 8` else oy); the same table holds for rotation 0 / 90 / 180 / 270 x flip_x with the offsets (0,0), (0,150), (150,0), (-40,3), (3,12), (12,12); delta arithmetic: source (5,7), anchor start (10,20), delta (3,4): flags (F,F) -> (3,4), (T,F) -> (3,-13), (F,T) -> (-5,4), (T,T) -> (-5,-13), with the flags swapped at 90 / 270; boundary: 4 OST units at 2 px per unit snap, 4.5 units (9 px) do not.

**Touch adaptation.** No keyboard or pointer. iPhone and iPad: Copy and Duplicate are items of the existing long-press sheet on a takeoff; "Paste Here" is an empty-space long-press item (ONE sheet with "Select All of Condition" when a condition is highlighted); an extra sheet item "Copy all of this condition on this page". No toolbar buttons (4.9). Paste target = the pressed point through `canvas_to_ost_point` (source anchor to the point), viewport centre as the Windows no-pointer fallback; Duplicate uses the plain offset, then the copy is selected and draggable. Pasted items form a small "pasted group": a set of uids in `SelectionState` (`Core/Domain/TakeoffSelection.hpp:43-52` gets `group_uids`, with `select_pasted_group`, the clearing rules (a tap elsewhere, a page switch, a Delete, Undo / Redo) and the Delete rule (Delete removes the whole group plus descendants, like Windows `delete_selected`), tested in `Config/Tests/Settings/test_takeoff_selection.cpp`); the next drag moves the group through `compute_group_translation_positions` (`drag_rules.hpp:97-103`) with 8 pt axis guides from `PasteAxisSnap.hpp` (no marquee, no Select All). Rules added at Windows `d26893c`: the guided drag is armed after "Paste Here" AND after Duplicate, starts from the pasted anchor (the copy of the item that gave the source anchor; for a Duplicate source + offset), swaps the guide flags on pages rotated 90 / 270, does not round the guided axis to the snap increment (CB-12), is single use and is dropped when the selection loses a member; a long press on a member of the group keeps the group (Copy and Duplicate act on the whole group, Delete removes group plus descendants) and a long press on a non-member clears the group and acts on that takeoff alone (N-M2-04). "Count as Negative Quantity" in the same sheet follows the Windows group rule (owner decision 3, 2026-11-28; Core `negative_toggle_for_press`, built in job 11): the M2 wiring passes the pasted uids as `group_uids`; on a member it sets ALL group takeoffs negative unless all are already negative (then all positive), with ONE undo entry; a group that contains a hole or attachment gets no item; the check mark shows only when all are negative. iPad: anchor every sheet; optional command C / V / D key commands at the end. Refusals use the existing "Not Saved" alert; the number-overflow refusal keeps the Windows title and text (`Paste` / `Cannot add N condition(s): the new condition numbers would reach X, past 2147483647, the highest condition number.`, `plan_view_action_handler.py:4667`, `condition_operations.py:56-62`). Redo re-inserts the same uids, refused with no change when a uid is taken. A paste commit (and a hole insert, 5.8) ends with `_refreshOverlays` like Place (`ViewController.mm:1349`), which is also what invalidates the snap index (5.9).

**Dependencies.** M1 (already delivered, re-checked at `368ae99`): `PageScaleTransform` with `rescale_entry_positions`, which already refuses a factor that is not finite and positive (CB-8), so `plan_paste_redo` is smaller than planned (redo after Set Scale); `allocate_condition_identities(count)` and `refuse_new_condition_numbers(highest, count)` (`ConditionFormRules.cpp:212`, with the singular and plural text); `ost_data::placement_tap_accepted` (`Core/Data/ProjectData.hpp:156`), the hidden-layer rule to reuse for CB-5 / N-M2-06; lock guard; UID limit; backout (5.8): a children-only clipboard (every copied item has a parent: area holes, attachments, count and linear children; "hole" in the Windows paste code means ANY child, `is_hole` = has a parent, `takeoff.py:135-137`) is hidden until Paste Backout exists, and a copy set that holds a hole whose parent is NOT in the set needs 5.8's `validate_paste` (the Windows `resolve_pasted_child_parents`, `plan_view_action_handler.py:5112-5140` -> `placement_mode.py:2169-2178`): until it exists `plan_paste` refuses such a set (nothing written, "Not Saved" alert) and the Windows tests `:15600` / `:15619` are ported in 5.8 step 3, not in 5.7; M3: pasted rows, extras and the regenerated GUID must export cleanly; M4: annotation paste (`PasteRefRemap`) ports with annotations; group selection beyond the pasted group is outside M1-M4.

**Risk.** Medium (Core / store low-medium; the group-selection UI slice high): a multi-row atomic write with parent remap and cross-bid condition copy in one transaction; single selection in a ~1,500-line `ViewController` whose new code has never run on a device; the clipboard must outlive the per-bid `ViewController`; redo with stable uids is an iOS design; the takeoff uid ceiling does not exist yet; UI is device-only.

**Size and order of work.** L: five steps (estimate: steps 1 and 2 medium, step 3 large because of the pasted-group drag, steps 4 and 5 small). Order: (1) Core clipboard + plan + translation + axis snap tests red, then green; (2) store (`insert_hole` first, for S6; then `paste_takeoffs`, same bid) + history entry + redo rescale tests; (3) long-press UI, pasted-group drag, device pack; (4) cross-bid (conditions copied, clipboard survives a bid change); (5) optional key commands. Size after Windows `d26893c` (A4 of job 9): the estimate of +240 to +300 for M2 stays, read as the upper half; `Config/Tests/Clipboard/test_paste_axis_snap.cpp` grows from a few planned cases to about 25 (pasted-anchor lifecycle 4, delta arithmetic 4, threshold and boundary 6, rotation / flip sweep 3 table-driven cases covering 8 x 6 rows, CB-12 3, group long-press rule 4, group delete 3), so 5.7 is about 110 to 120 host cases, not 100. The negative group rule is already built and tested in job 11 (9 cases, not part of the M2 estimate); the M2 wiring adds about 3 to 5 cases of its own (the pasted uids reach `negative_toggle_for_press` as the group, a long press on a member keeps the group, a non-member acts alone, a group with a hole or attachment hides the item), so 5.7 is about 113 to 125 host cases. All new rules live in S5-owned files (6.5); M2 is not started.

CB-2 `plan_view_action_handler.py:4547-4554`, `:5355-5357` - the paste anchor depends on Python set order -> deterministic: first regular takeoff in project list order. CB-4 Cut is listed but always disabled (`toolbar_state_coordinator.py:251-253`) -> no Cut. CB-7 almost every refusal is silent (only the number overflow has a message) -> "Not Saved" alert for every refused write. CB-8 `page_scale_transform.py:18-19`, `:23-30` - the rescale factor is 0.0 for a zero target ratio and negative / NaN for odd scales -> refuse the redo (history stays) unless the factor is finite and positive. CB-9 `identity_allocation.py:5-8`, `takeoff_operations.py:504-507` - no takeoff uid ceiling -> refuse the whole paste before writing past 2147483647. CB-11 `takeoff_operations.py:36-45` - a same-bid paste keeps `BidZoneUID` and `TypGroup*` of the source (low confidence whether intended) -> kept (iOS Delete-undo does the same, `ProjectStore+Takeoffs.cpp:364-365`) and flagged. Windows d26893c: only the second half of CB-3 (guides align the wrong pair) is fixed, by `df78f67` (see the new rules above); CB-12 was new there and is FIXED on Windows at `d1c430a` (item (5) above, an M2 input); the BO-12 clarification belongs to 5.8; the other rows are unchanged (section 8, last column).

**Tests-first plan (M2).** Port red first: translation (offset 2.5 / 1.0, mouse (15,4) - anchor (5,6) = (10,-2), no anchor), copy no-alias and second-copy-replaces, parent / hole remap with nested generations and a cycle refused, redo rescale [7.333, 14, 71.333, 14] and the curved trailing value with 20 round trips, cross-bid conditions once and redo reuses them, overflow text at 2147483647, six bid-scoped columns NULL cross-bid and kept same-bid, GUID regenerated, axis snap (8 pt, boundary, both axes), Count as Negative on the pasted group (wiring: the pasted uids are the group, member and non-member press, hole or attachment hides the item, one undo entry; the rule itself is host-tested in `Und09_NegativeGroup`). Data-safety (new): locked bid -> 0 rows, uid reservation past 2147483647 refused before any write, a taken uid on redo refuses with no change, factor 0 / NaN / negative refuses the redo, `TempDb::block` on a later insert rolls back the whole paste including copied conditions, pasted BidAreaUID unassigned, `snapshot_all` equal after every refusal.

---

### 5.8 Backout (hole) modes: Backout mode and Paste Backout (M, risk medium)

**What Windows does.** A backout is a takeoff whose `parent_uid` names an AREA takeoff (an area child of an area parent is a real hole; attachments are also children). **Backout mode** = Place mode of the parent's condition plus a backout state, entered with exactly ONE selected visible non-hole area takeoff (>= 6 position values). A drag draws a rectangle (clamped to the last valid corner), taps draw a polygon; each hole must be inside the parent, clear of sibling area holes and must not push an attachment out (`_check_hole_overlap`). One takeoff is inserted with `parent_uid` and the parent's condition; one undo entry. **Paste Backout** (paste with a children-only clipboard) previews the copied group as green / red dashed outlines and commits only when ALL sources are valid (first-fit parent, internal parents ordered, no collisions), in one transaction with parent remapping.

**Windows modules.**

S5's `_applySelectionState:` (5.7) calls it once as well, so every change that goes through that function (tap, Delete, Undo / Redo, page switch: `ViewController.mm:403`, `:423`, `:1013-1057`) reaches it, as does every mode change; S7 edits neither function), `FloatingModeButton.mm`

**Touch adaptation.** Entry on iPhone and iPad: a contextual "Backout" chip that appears while a single valid parent area is selected and stays visible in its active state (tap = leave Backout) for as long as Backout mode lasts: the new hole becomes the selection after each insert (Windows `plan_view_action_handler.py:3609`) but the mode depends only on the parent and its condition (`view.py:3250-3261`) (the 2D bar already holds six buttons); the chip is the leading half of a chip row directly above the floating mode button (`ViewController.mm:162-172`; the Snap chip of 5.9 is the trailing half, so the two never overlap, and the row clears the angle toggle and the placement-undo button that already sit beside the button, `:174-235`), plus an entry in the long-press sheet on iPad (anchored; the "Count as Negative Quantity" item of the same sheet follows the group rule of 5.7: no item for a selection that holds a hole, so a pasted area with its hole has none, while a single pressed hole still offers it on iOS, a recorded divergence). Backout is a sub-state of Place mode; another FAB mode or another condition leaves it. Draw: pan = rectangle, taps = polygon points, close within 12 pt, existing undo-last-point and long-press cancel; no loupe; status text ("outside the area", "overlaps a backout", "clamped to the area"); refusals get a red flash + light haptic (Windows is silent in several). Preview: dashed outline, parent unchanged until commit. Validation uses a `HierarchyModel` built once per gesture (at touch-down) and rebuilt after every commit, undo, redo and page change, never per pan event: the model holds references and raw pointers into `ProjectData::takeoffs` (`hierarchy_validation.hpp:33-50`), which a hole insert invalidates. Paste Backout: no hover, so a draggable ghost (green / red dashed per source) with Place (enabled only when ALL sources are valid) and Cancel; lifting a finger never commits; iPad floating pill, iPhone bottom bar. Backout lost involuntarily (parent deleted, page change) leaves Place mode too and says so.

**Dependencies.** Backout mode needs only existing Core; Paste Backout needs the clipboard (5.7: copy + descendants + extras + source bid) and the new hierarchy insert; M3: export keeps holes, parent links and cross-condition holes. No new library.

**Risk.** Medium (mode low-medium, Paste Backout medium-high): the Windows DB layer does not re-validate a hole, so Core re-validates at commit; Qt-clipper residue and an unpinned Qt `contains()` boundary rule (boundary-exact holes on a snap grid are the case to test); new paste transaction + multi-item undo; takeoff `UID` / `ParentUID` are 32-bit in Access (`database_creator.py:467`, `:474`); all UI is compile-checked only, device checks needed.

**Size and order of work.** M (estimates): mode ~300 Core lines + 250 Objective-C++; Paste Backout ~400 Core + store + history + ghost UI after 5.7. Order: (1) validators + rectangle clamp tests red; (2) Backout mode (chip, draw, commit through `insert_hole` of S5's `ProjectStore+Paste.cpp`: lock, UID limit, Core re-validation of BO-1 before the write); (3) Paste Backout after 5.7 step 2; (4) device pack rows.

**Windows oddities and the safe behaviour kept** (register BO-1 to BO-11): BO-1 `takeoff_operations.py:482-501`, `bid_owned_identity.py:53-65` - no hole re-validation at the write boundary -> re-run the Core hole / parent validation right before the write and refuse with "Not Saved". BO-2 `placement_mode.py:2253-2296` - paste takes the first fitting parent in dict order -> deterministic project order, chosen parent highlighted (N-M2-12). BO-3 `view.py:3272` vs `placement_mode.py:2253-2262` - Backout mode refuses a parent on a hidden layer, Paste Backout does not -> refuse for both. BO-4 `placement_mode.py:2030-2034`, `:2253-2262` - `_check_hole_overlap` skips containment for a parent with fewer than 6 values (fails open) -> refuse any parent with < 6 or an odd number of values everywhere. BO-5 `placement_mode.py:2007-2016`, `:2126-2135` - any child with >= 6 values is treated as a hole -> area siblings only. BO-6 silent refusals -> flash, haptic, status text. BO-7 `view.py:6316-6333` - Qt `contains` boundary unspecified -> one boundary-inclusive rule = `path_is_inside`. BO-8 `view.py:3280-3287` - losing the context leaves the area place session armed -> leave Place mode and say so. BO-10 `plan_view_action_handler.py:3529-3541` - a cross-condition pasted hole survives deletion of the parent's condition as a positive area of its own condition -> kept (iOS mirrors the delete, `ProjectData.hpp:55-69`), documented; Backout mode never creates cross-condition holes. BO-11 UID / ParentUID are 32-bit -> guard at 2147483647 (4.2). BO-12 (clarification of Windows d26893c, unchanged code, `view.py:6440-6521`, `takeoff.py:135-137`, `plan_view_action_handler.py:4639-4657`, `placement_mode.py:2196-2300`) - "hole" in the paste code means ANY child (`is_hole` = has a parent): a children-only clipboard (area holes, attachments, count and linear children) goes to Paste Backout; a source is valid with at least 6 values, at least 4 for a linear and exactly 2 for an attachment or count; one unusable source refuses the whole set; non-area children are validated as "symbols" -> read "holes-only" in the Windows names as "children-only"; `validate_paste` handles or explicitly refuses attachment / count / linear children with the "Not Saved" alert: decide in step 3 of the order of work. Windows d26893c: BO-1 to BO-11 are unchanged (section 8, last column).

**Tests-first plan (M2).** Port red: rectangle minimum and last-valid clamp (`test_placement_mode.py:1715`, `:1737`), click = polygon (`:2016`, `:2042`), `_check_hole_overlap` (`:2438-2479`), paste validation (`:1748-1813`), valid parent / candidate / context rules (`test_view.py:22460-22665`), centroid (15/6, 23/6). Data-safety (new): parent with < 6 or odd values refused (BO-4), area siblings only (BO-5), boundary hole accepted (BO-7), hidden-layer parent refused for both modes (BO-3), hole insert on a locked bid -> 0 rows, uid limit.

### 5.9 Snap to PDF lines and takeoff endpoints (L, risk medium-high)

**What Windows does.** One point per cursor position is resolved in this order: takeoff outlines (with crossings), PDF vector lines (with crossings), grid node, raw cursor (`placement_mode.py:786-817`); then a right-angle pull (areas only), the angle snap and, for grid points only, a length snap (the right-angle pull and the 8 + 2 snap preferences are in M2 because Windows keeps them in the same chain and `PORTING_PLAN.md:91` lists them with this feature; no other snap tool is added). The index is a self-contained C++17 class (`cpp_extensions/src/snap/snap_index.*`, STL only, copyable into `Core/Geometry`). "Takeoff endpoints" means the corners and edges of the RENDERED outlines: linear = two border lines at +-thickness/2 (not the centreline), area edges, count shape outlines (circles as 32 segments). Curves never snap. Takeoffs of a condition on a hidden layer are skipped (`placement_mode.py:570-573`), and the PDF source exists only while the page image layer is visible and the page image is a PDF (`:431-449`; with the overlay shown it is the overlay PDF, M4). Dragging existing takeoffs uses only grid and angle snap (already on iOS). The hard part on iOS is PDF line extraction: Windows uses PDFium, iOS only has CGPDF.

**Windows modules.**
- `cpp_extensions/src/snap/snap_index.hpp:9-54`, `snap_index.cpp:9-340` - `SnapIndex`: kinds NONE -1, GRID 0, ENDPOINT 1, MIDPOINT 2, PERPENDICULAR 3, INTERSECTION 4; a crossing wins, then nearest ENDPOINT over MIDPOINT over PERPENDICULAR regardless of distance (`:327-338`); at most 32768 pairs
- `cpp_extensions/src/pdf/pdf_renderer.cpp:530-626` (`extract_path_segments`: lines and closes, curves ignored), `:627-1007` (`extract_path_items`: forms to depth 16, Bezier flattening)
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:84-279` - `PdfSnapSource`, `pdf_raw_point_to_page_point` (/Rotate), overlay mapping, `extract_pdf_snap_segments` (visible-box origin subtracted, times `scale_ratio/72`)
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:290-417`, `:725-834` - `_snap_angle`, right-angle target, angle and length snap (`_snap_angle_for_placement :380-396`), the priority chain `_placement_snap_from_scene :786`, marker for line kinds only
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/placement_mode.py:419-697` - index lifecycle, cache key (scale ratio and overlay geometry), takeoff outline segment builder, async PDF index build
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/pdf/pdf_visible_origin.py:9-21` - visible box origin (returns (0,0) on any read problem)
- `ost_visualizer/application/dtos/snap_preferences_dto.py:5-50`, `domain/entities/config.py:31-34`, `:75-84`, `:263-294` - eight preferences (4 flags, 4 thresholds, default on and 8) and two angle steps (15 and 0; valid set 0,1,2,3,4,5,10,15,30,45,90), thresholds 0..100 else 8
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/components/plan_view/components/drag_handler.py:261-264`, `:293-342` - handle drags use only grid + angle

**Windows tests that pin it.** `tests/integration/native/test_snap_index_grid.py` (7) and `test_snap_index_intersections.py` (15: crossing beats a closer endpoint, T-junctions stay endpoints, pair cap 32768); `tests/integration/native/test_pdf_path_items.py` (18: the contract of the extractor); `tests/presentation/components/plan_view/components/test_placement_mode.py` (`SnapSegmentCacheTests` `:456-1599`: ~58 snap tests; `PdfSnapVisibleBoxOriginTests :2627`, `:2661`); `tests/domain/aggregates/test_config_aggregate.py:315-365`. About 108 Windows tests define it; roughly 80 port directly as host tests (SnapIndex 22, takeoff segments ~10, chain / angle / right-angle ~25, mapping ~6, preferences ~9, pure collector ~8); only the threading and Qt marker tests port as intent: the cache-key, retry and completeness tests of `SnapSegmentCacheTests` port as host tests of `snap_source_cache`; the CGPDF scanner wrapper itself can be checked only on a device or CI (estimate 100 host cases).

**iOS today / iOS files touched.** `Core/Geometry/placement_snap.hpp/.cpp` (grid and angle, 16 host tests in `Config/Tests/Geometry/test_placement_snap.cpp`; `SnapKind::None` / `Grid` `:44-48`), `PlanView/Components/PlanView+Placement.mm:50-66` (`_placementPointAtCanvas`: grid only), `:300-461`, `ViewController.mm:674-683` (`_handleSnapToggle`, the round angle toggle = the Shift stand-in), `DragController.mm:23-43`, `Core/Geometry/coord_transform.hpp`, `takeoff_footprint.hpp:40,52`, `ProjectData.hpp:79-101`, `ProjectStore_Internal.hpp:114-153` (preferences), `PlanView/Components/PDFCanvasView.mm:5-6`, `:22-34` (CGPDF only; no PDFium in the repo). Missing: `SnapIndex`, PDF vector extraction, takeoff outline segments, right-angle snap and indicator, preferences and UI, marker, hover. New / changed:
- NEW Core: `Core/Geometry/snap_index.hpp/.cpp` (copy of the Windows class, STL only), `takeoff_snap_segments.hpp/.cpp`, `pdf_vectors.hpp/.cpp` (mapping, matrices, event-driven path collector with cap and partial flag), `snap_source_cache.hpp/.cpp` (the pure part of the index lifecycle, `placement_mode.py:419-697`: cache key from page uid, layer, file, mtime, page index, sheet size, overlay rect / rotation / units and scale ratio; dirty flags; "complete" only when nothing failed or was truncated (SN-4, SN-5); one retry after an invalidation; superseded requests dropped), `Core/Domain/SnapPreferences.hpp/.cpp`; `placement_snap.*` grows (`SnapKind` six values, `resolve_placement_snap`, right-angle, area endpoint)
- NEW store: `Components/ProjectStore+Settings.cpp` + `ProjectStore_Settings.hpp` (typed preferences on `_AppPreferences`, 4.13); NEW bridge `Services/ImportService+SnapSettings.h/.mm`
- NEW `Services/PdfVectorService.h/.mm` (`CGPDFScanner` wrapper, background queue, own `CGPDFDocument`, cache, cancel), `PlanView/Components/PlanView+Snap.mm` (queue hand-over, marker, offset cursor, hover; no decisions), `Controllers/SnapSettingsViewController.h/.mm`, `Controllers/ViewController/Components/ViewController+SnapSettings.mm` (the Snap chip: created, shown or hidden and presented from here, 6.5); CHANGED `PlanView.h` (S7's marker block: ONE public method that marks the takeoff part of the snap index stale; `ViewController.mm` imports only `PlanView.h`, never `PlanView_Internal.h` (`ViewController_Internal.h:6`), so without this declaration the one line at the top of `_refreshOverlays` is an undeclared selector, a compile error under ARC), `PlanView.mm`, `PlanView+Placement.mm`, `ViewController.mm` (only that one line), `PlanView_Internal.h`; `UndoRedoController` is not edited by S7 and there is no new delegate method: the takeoff part of the snap index is marked stale by ONE line that S7 adds at the top of `_refreshOverlays` (`ViewController.mm:776`), the refresh that every place (`:1349`), move (`:1190`, `:1293`), delete, undo and redo (`undoRedoDidModify`, `:1028`), layer (`ViewController+Layers.mm:116`, `:313`) and condition style change (`ViewController+Conditions.mm:178`, `:244`, `:306`) already ends with, and that a paste and a hole insert end with like Place (5.7, 5.8); the PDF part of the index is keyed by page, scale and layer (`snap_source_cache`) and is not touched by it (6.5)
- NEW tests: `Config/Tests/Geometry/test_snap_index.cpp`, `test_takeoff_snap_segments.cpp`, `test_pdf_vectors.cpp`, `test_snap_source_cache.cpp`; `Config/Tests/Settings/test_snap_preferences.cpp`

**Touch adaptation.** There is no hover and the finger covers the target. iPhone and iPad: a quick tap places at the SNAPPED point; touch-and-hold shows the marker and an offset cursor about 56 pt above a finger (none for Pencil or pointer), release places; a `UIHoverGestureRecognizer` serves mouse and trackpad, and Apple Pencil hover on the iPad models that support it (iPadOS 16.1+; the deployment target is 16.0, so the Pencil path is checked at run time and on a device). Thresholds stay at the Windows defaults (8, range 0..100) and zoom is the precision tool. The round toggle stays as the Shift stand-in and a hardware Shift is read too. Marker: square, for line kinds only, plus a light haptic when the target changes. Settings: a Snap chip (created, shown or hidden and presented by S7's `ViewController+SnapSettings.mm`, anchored to the floating mode button `fab`, 6.5) on the trailing half of a chip row directly above the floating mode button while a placement mode is active (no toolbar slot, 4.9; the Backout chip of 5.8 is the leading half of the same row, so the two never overlap, and the row clears the angle toggle and the placement-undo button beside the button, `ViewController.mm:174-235`) opens a popover on iPad (WITH a source anchor) and a half sheet on iPhone. The PDF index is built on a background queue (own document, current-page cache, segment cap with a partial flag, no snap until ready).

**Dependencies.** M1 Set Scale: `sf1` / `sf2` are part of the index cache key, so a scale change invalidates it; settings store (4.13); M2 backout drawing runs through the same `resolve_placement_snap` as area placement (Windows `handle_place_press` calls `_placement_snap_from_scene` for backout points, `placement_mode.py:1644`), so S6 and S7 share `PlanView+Placement.mm` (6.5); clipboard paste does not use it (raw pointer, `plan_view_action_handler.py:5343`; Paste Backout uses the grid-snapped cursor, `placement_mode.py:2156-2157`); M4 annotation placement reuses the resolver and the overlay PDF snap source needs overlay drawing. No new library (copy `snap_index.*`; do NOT add PDFium).

**Risk.** Medium-high. Low: the `SnapIndex` port, outline builder, priority chain, angle and right-angle rules, preferences (pure C++, Windows tests port directly). HIGH: PDF extraction on CGPDF must reproduce the PDFium segment list from raw operators, forms, matrices, `/Rotate` and crop box; the scanner wrapper cannot run on the Windows host; huge CAD pages cost time and memory; a mapping error silently shifts every snap. Medium: touch interaction and gesture conflicts, iPad popover anchoring, float32 hit precision.

**Size and order of work.** L. Order: (1) `SnapIndex` + takeoff segments + chain / angle / right-angle tests red -> green; (2) preferences + store; (3) takeoff snap wired into placement, marker, offset cursor, settings sheet; (4) PDF mapping + pure collector tests, then the CGPDF wrapper and index lifecycle; (5) device pack rows (a device-only check of PDF-line alignment on rotated / cropped sheets).

**Windows oddities and the safe behaviour kept** (register SN-1 to SN-11): SN-1 `placement_mode.py:380-396` - a line / endpoint / crossing hit is angle-snapped AGAIN (only the length rounding is skipped), so the placed point can lie off the endpoint that was hit (the Windows preview already draws its handle at the FINAL point, `:1488-1489`, `:1628`; the snap marker exists only for first points, `:1455`, `:1493`, `:1636`) -> Windows order kept; on iOS the marker and the preview both show the FINAL point (N-M2-14, register SN-1). SN-2 `placement_mode.py:344-370` - the right-angle snap is cancelled by the default 15 degree angle snap (pinned) -> ported as pinned, documented, device-checked. SN-4 `pdf_visible_origin.py:15-20` - a failed visible-box read returns (0,0) and the index is still marked complete (lines shifted) -> an extraction failure: empty index, logged, one retry. SN-5 `placement_mode.py:103`, `:117-123`; `pdf_renderer.cpp:530-626` - a 250000-item cap whose truncation flag is ignored, and an uncapped other path -> one cap (2,000,000 segments) with a partial flag, never "complete" when truncated. SN-6 no colour / alpha / optional-content check -> parity first, skip unpainted `n` paths. SN-7 `config_aggregate.py:209-217` - one malformed value resets the WHOLE config -> per-field validation. SN-9 float32 hits (`snap_index.hpp:9-15`) -> keep float32 inside the index, recompute takeoff endpoint hits in double. SN-11 curves never snap (pinned) -> parity. Windows d26893c: SN-1 to SN-11 are unchanged except the labels of SN-3: the two angle labels now read "Mouse unpressed angle:" / "Mouse pressed angle:" (trailing colon, `presentation/texts.py:182-185`) while the code still reads the Shift key (still misnamed), and "Enable Intelligent Paste" became "Enable intelligent paste" (`:169`); `placement_mode.py`, `snap_index.*` and `pdf_renderer.cpp` are byte-identical (section 8, last column).

**Tests-first plan (M2).** Port red: SnapIndex grid and intersection corpora (22), outline segments incl. 32-gon circles and the 0.0-thickness default border, priority chain, angle / right-angle (pinned `:1173-1188`, `:1234-1249`), mapping and /Rotate, visible-box origin, preferences ranges (`test_config_aggregate.py:315-365`). Data-safety (new): failed extraction = empty index, truncation = partial flag, one malformed preference resets only its field.

**MILESTONE M3 - exports**

### 5.10 Summary CSV export (M, risk medium)

**What Windows does.** One CSV file: no header, UTF-8 without BOM, CRLF, every cell quoted, 14 or 15 cells per row, quantities as `str(int(round(v)))` (half-even), height as raw inches with 5 decimals, 7 sort branches for the 8 combinations of the three grouping switches page / type / area (`summary_csv_export_service.py:283-301`; page + type + area uses the page + area branch, so the type key is ignored); default grouping type + area. It is built from the Summary tree (`ConditionSummaryService`), which iOS does not have, and needs the bid areas (names, sequence). An optional "drop condition elevation" switch strips the ` @T ` / ` @B ` suffix from names and reports name collisions.

**Windows modules.**

New / changed:
- NEW Core: `Core/Domain/BidArea.hpp`, `ConditionSummary.{hpp,cpp}` (build + sort), `Core/Domain/SummaryCsvOptions.{hpp,cpp}` (typed, per-field validated; defaults type + area, drop-elevation off; 4.13), `Core/Import/HtmlUnescape.{hpp,cpp}` + generated `HtmlEntityData.hpp`, `Core/Export/summary_csv.{hpp,cpp}`; `strip_elevation_suffix` / `elevation_name_collisions` in `PositionParser.*`; `py_rstrip` in `TextRules.*`; `UomService.hpp` gains a condition filter

- NEW `Services/ExportService` `exportSummaryCSV` and `loadSummaryCsvOptions` / `saveSummaryCsvOptions` (a thin bridge to `load_summary_csv_options` / `update_summary_csv_options`; it gets the database path from `ImportService_Internal.h`, 4.4 item 3); NEW `Controllers/SummaryExportOptionsViewController.{h,mm}` (calls the bridge, never the store); CHANGED `ViewController+Export.mm` (Export sheet item and messages)
- NEW tests: `Config/Tests/Export/test_summary_csv*.cpp`, `Quantities/test_condition_summary.cpp`, `Parsers/test_elevation_strip.cpp`, `Parsers/test_html_unescape.cpp`, `Persistence/test_load_areas.cpp`, `Settings/test_summary_csv_options.cpp`, `Fixtures/summary_csv_oracle.txt` (golden files generated from the read-only Windows tree with Python 3.11; no CMake or `project.yml` edit)

**Touch adaptation.** There is no Summary tab, so the grouping comes from a small option sheet (three switches + the drop-elevation switch; form sheet on iPad, medium detent on iPhone; no popover anchor needed), defaults "by type + by area", remembered in `_AppPreferences` (4.13). File name fixed by the app (sanitised bid name + " Summary.csv", temp sub-folder) and delivered with `UIActivityViewController` (anchored as in `ViewController+Export.mm:93-94`); temp file deleted in the completion handler; result alert after the share completes; data copied on the main thread, file built on a background queue with a spinner; no locale-dependent formatting. **Windows texts kept verbatim:** `No Data` / `No summary rows are available to export.` (`export_handler.py:355-360`); `Export Complete` / `Successfully exported Summary to {filename}` (`:347-353`); the collision note, reworded at Windows d26893c: "Note: after dropping elevations, 1 condition name became identical (<shown>). Each condition was still exported as its own entry; none were merged." / "N condition names became identical (...)", "X and N more" for a long list (`:46-64`; the `49a2d59` text was "Note: dropping elevations made N condition names identical ..."); `Export Error` / the result sentence ONLY, without the `49a2d59` prefix "Error creating Summary CSV export: " (Windows d26893c, texts `EXPORT_SERVICE_MESSAGE_*` in `ost_visualizer/shared/texts.py`): a write failure reads "The file could not be written. <reason>" with the reasons "The destination folder was not found. Choose another folder and try again." / "Access to the file was denied. Check that you have permission to use the file and its folder." / "There is not enough space on the disk. Free up space and try again." / "The network location is not available. Check the network connection and try again.", else "The file could not be written. Please ensure you have write permissions to the destination folder and try again."; any other exception reads "An unexpected error stopped the export. Please try again or choose a different destination; if the problem continues, contact support."; the default file name is "<bid> Summary.csv" ("Bid" when the bid has no name). Take these from the Windows texts module when 5.10 is built (M3 input; owner decision EXP-21 of 2026-11-28: the Windows wording is adopted as it is, including the "write permissions" sentence although iOS has no destination folder dialog; the built Core module and the rule are in 4.15).

**Dependencies.** None on M1 / M2 for the logic. M3 file ownership: 5.10 builds the share plumbing and the Export-sheet menu skeleton (one item per export in the order CSV, OST, OSP, PDF). 5.11 and 5.12 add their code in their own category files (`Services/ExportService+Bid.{h,mm}`, `Services/ExportService+Pdf.{h,mm}`, `ViewController+ExportBid.mm`, `ViewController+ExportPdf.mm`) and touch `ViewController+Export.mm` only for their menu lines (two items for 5.11, OST and OSP; one for 5.12; the rule of 4.4). The raw-bid store function gets its own header `ProjectStore_RawExport.hpp` with one include line in `ProjectStore.hpp`. It builds first in M3 the pieces OST / OSP reuse: `strip_elevation_suffix`, the HTML unescape table, the collision note, the share-sheet plumbing (4.13). Needs the read-only `BidAreas` load (N-M3-02: this conflicts with the 2026-11-16 decision "areas are not surfaced" and the owner's out-of-scope "bid areas"; there is NO area UI or editing). No third-party library.

**Risk.** Medium: the file is a data contract for external templates (column drift breaks silently); many sort branches and Python semantics (half-even rounding, big integers, text-compared uids); HTML unescape fidelity needs a full generated table; areas are not loaded on iOS, so a silent fallback would write a wrong file; share sheet, option sheet and iPad anchors are device-only.

**Size and order of work.** M (estimate: ~1,300 Core lines, ~250 Objective-C++ lines, ~100 ported + 40 new tests). Order: (1) elevation strip / unescape / collision tests red -> green; (2) summary tree + sort tests (oracle golden); (3) CSV writer + oracle files; (4) areas read (only after your answer to N-M3-02); (5) options sheet + export item + share; (6) device pack rows.

**Windows oddities and the safe behaviour kept** (register C-1 to C-16): C-1 `summary_csv_export_service.py:326-330` - only the top-level folder is written, a nested folder never appears -> replicate (the column layout is a contract), list for the Windows owner. C-2 `:249-253`, `:348-349`, `:364-365` - the optional area cell (8th cell, index 7) exists only when area grouping is off or page grouping is on (the default type + area layout has 14 cells without it); where it exists it is the constant "(unassigned)" on every normal row and "Total" on a multi-area total row -> replicate. C-3 `:373-375` vs `application/utils/quantity_display.py:4-9` - every quantity is `str(int(round(v)))` whatever the unit, so 0.45 m3 becomes 0 in a metric bid while the Summary tab shows two decimals -> two decimals below 100 for metric units, integers otherwise, a DELIBERATE difference (N-M3-04). C-6 `settings_reader.py:392-398`, `project_read_service.py:138-143` - a BidAreas read error is swallowed, the export succeeds with every row "(unassigned)" -> refuse the export. C-7 `summary_csv_export_service.py:161-171` - the collision check ignores `multi_area_total` nodes (built only when area grouping is OFF and a condition spans two or more areas, `condition_summary_service.py:320-367`; the default type + area layout has only `condition` nodes) -> count them, each condition once; the tests use a layout without the area switch. C-8 default file name not sanitised -> `sanitize_filename` + 255 UTF-8 bytes. C-11 type of a condition without a type is "Unknown" ungrouped but "(unassigned)" grouped (`condition_summary_service.py:436` vs `:487-491`) -> "(unassigned)" in both, "Unknown" for a dangling type uid (Windows d26893c fixed this: `condition_summary_service.py:446-447`, type name = the loader's name if the type uid is set, else "(unassigned)", so a typeless condition reads "(unassigned)" in its row, its type group and the CSV and a type uid with no `CdnTypes` row keeps "Unknown"; derive "no type" from the empty uid, never from the name "Unknown"; test names `test_a_condition_without_a_type_reads_unassigned_in_its_row_and_group`, `test_a_dangling_type_keeps_the_name_the_loader_gave_it`). C-15 `elevation.py:108-115` - a numeric entity of more than 4300 digits fails the whole export -> U+FFFD, no failure. C-9 / C-10 no BOM and no neutralising of cells starting with `= + - @` -> no BOM (as Windows); a TEXT cell that starts with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, TAB or CR gets a leading `'`, number cells are never touched (a deliberate difference, N-M3-05).

**Texts (owner decision EXP-21, job 11).** All alert, progress and note texts of this feature are the Windows sentences of section 4.15; the writer returns a typed result and the Core text module builds the sentence; no raw error ever reaches the alert.

### 5.11 OST and OSP export with a Windows round trip (B-009 in `PORTING_BLOCKERS.md`; L, risk HIGH)

**What Windows does.** `.ost`: attributes-only XML, CRLF, no BOM, no declaration, Version 3.2.0, fixed attribute and row order, `BidAreaConditions` recomputed from takeoffs, written from RAW table rows with Windows normalisations (condition UOM1-3 normalised for the bid's measurement system, `ost_exporter.py:529-545` called at `:662`, iOS `UomService.hpp:392`; omit-if-empty columns `:463-469`, `:548-557`; ExternalID defaults to 0 `:663-664`, `:795-796`; YESNO as 1 / 0, floats whole -> int else `%.15f` trimmed, datetime "Y M D h m s", NUL dropped, latin-1 for `BidTexts` / `BidCallOuts` Name, UTF-8 otherwise), after an export-side prune of orphans. `.osp`: an MSZIP cabinet (Windows FCI, ONE folder, UTF-8 flagged names): `<bid>.ost`, `BidTrans.xml`, `TempImages!.tmp\<drawing>`.

**Windows modules.**
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/exporters/ost_exporter.py:36-493` (attribute-order and null tables), `:496-630` (helpers: `_escape_xml_attr :598-608`), `:637-702` (`export`: prepare + validate, atomic temp + fsync + replace), `:704-1078` (sections, per-condition `BidAreaConditions`, 17 page sections, referenced global rows only)
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/schema_contract.py:3-47`, `:55-79` - section lists, `CopyTimeStamp` -> `CopyTimestamp`, `singular()`
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/raw_bid_integrity.py:121-272`, `:376-489`, `:645-728` - `BID_RELATIONSHIPS`, `validate_raw_bid_integrity`, `prepare_raw_bid_data_for_export` (`:645`: clear dangling selected page, silently prune orphans in a loop, raise on a fatal takeoff graph)
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/parsers/ost_serializer.py:12-51`, `domain/services/ost_text_values.py:6-30`, `infrastructure/mdb/components/serialization.py:5-87` - cell formats and storage encodings
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/components/bid_data_reader.py:334-352`, `:381-467` - `get_raw_bid_data`
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/osp_exporter.py:50-149`, `:165-290`, `:255-257` (`_safe_path_component`), `:292-305` - 4 progress steps, missing-drawing refusal, member names, collision names (sha1[:16]), `BidTrans.xml`
- `cpp_extensions/src/cab/cab_compressor.cpp:446-494`, `:294`, `:74-82`, `:495-545` - FCI writer (MSZIP, one folder, UTF name flag, DOS dates) and FDI reader
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/handlers/export_handler.py:368-401`, `:403-514` - flow (flush persistence first), progress, messages
- `ost_visualizer/domain/services/elevation.py:108-159` - `strip_raw_condition_elevations`
- `ost_visualizer/infrastructure/mdb/importers/ost_importer.py:158-199`, `:427-482`, `osp_importer.py:22-29`, `:81-101`, `:154-189`, `:367-410` - the acceptance criteria on the Windows import side

**Windows tests that pin it.** `tests/infrastructure/mdb/exporters/test_ost_exporter.py` (72: byte layout, golden attribute order `:2461`, empty bid `:1471`, escaping `:1090`, area quantities `:589-1058`, atomic write); `tests/presentation/visualization/exporters/test_osp_exporter.py` (28: member naming and order, collisions `:384`, missing-drawing list `:339`, `BidTrans.xml` `:416`); `tests/integration/import_export/test_osp_roundtrip.py:41-135`, `tests/integration/native/test_osp_cab.py:93-121`; `tests/integration/import_export/test_condition_elevation_export.py` (7); `tests/infrastructure/parsers/test_ost_serializer.py`, `tests/domain/services/test_ost_text_values.py`, `tests/integration/persistence/test_raw_text_parity.py`; `tests/infrastructure/mdb/test_raw_bid_integrity.py` (~10 export-side); `tests/helpers/mdb/ost_attribute_order_golden.py` (a transcription of the exporter, NOT an independent oracle). About 135 portable (estimate 180 host cases); ~20 rely on Python mocks and must be re-expressed through an injected file system, clock and GUID source; the 2 Windows-path tests of `test_osp_cab.py` stay Windows-side.

**iOS today / iOS files touched.** The whole reverse direction exists: `Core/Import/OstXmlParser.{hpp,cpp}` (strict parser, `CopyTimestamp` mapping `.cpp:9-18`), `RawBidIntegrity.*` (validate, `resolve_takeoff_graph`; no export prune), `OspPackage.*`, `CabMemberName`, `Digest` (SHA-1 / 256), `TextRules`, `OspFileSystem` (injectable, but read / stage only: no write, rename, fsync or free-space call; it is left unchanged), `CabExtractor` (zlib MSZIP reader; no deflate in Core), the TEST-ONLY CAB builder `Config/Tests/Import/CabTestSupport.hpp:56-211`, the Windows DDL (`OstSchema`), `UomService` quantities, `ExportService` + share sheet, `export_filename`, `ImportService.mm:172-187` (drawings staged to `Documents/OST/<package>/` with an ABSOLUTE `ImagePath`), `Config/Info.plist:54-90` (.ost / .osp types). Missing: SQLite-to-raw-rows reader, OST writer, export-side prune with a report, a streaming CAB writer in Core, the OSP assembler, UI. New / changed:
- NEW `Core/Export/CabWriter.{hpp,cpp}` (streaming MSZIP, 32 KiB blocks with dictionary, checksums, UTF flag, backslash member names, size limits), `OstWriter.{hpp,cpp}`, `OstRawNormalize.{hpp,cpp}` (including the UOM normalisation, the omit-if-empty columns and the ExternalID default named in "What Windows does", and the Employees scrub of N-M3-10), `OstAttributeOrder.hpp` (tables of `ost_exporter.py:36-493`), `OspWriter.{hpp,cpp}` (image planning, collisions, `BidTrans.xml`, atomic replace, injected clock / GUID), `RawBidExport.{hpp,cpp}` (`prepare_for_export` with a removed-rows report)
- NEW `Core/Export/ExportFileSystem.hpp` + `StdExportFileSystem` (open_write / write / flush + fsync / rename-over / remove / free space), injected into `OstWriter` and `OspWriter`; the test fake implements it
- NEW `Components/ProjectStore+RawExport.cpp` + `ProjectStore_RawExport.hpp` (`read_raw_bid` in one DEFERRED read transaction: `OstDatabase` has only `begin_transaction()` = BEGIN IMMEDIATE, "every caller is a writer" (`OstDatabase.hpp:116-118`), so a read through it would hold the write lock for the whole export and the UI writes would wait the 5 s busy timeout (`OstDatabase.hpp:104`) and fail; this feature therefore CHANGES `Core/Persistence/OstDatabase.{hpp,cpp}` and adds `begin_read_transaction()` (BEGIN DEFERRED; the database is WAL, best effort, `OstDatabase.cpp:202`, so the reader sees a stable snapshot and a concurrent writer is not blocked) with a host test in `Config/Tests/Persistence/test_db_api.cpp` that a second connection can write while the read transaction is open and the reader still sees its snapshot); `OstSchema` XML-direction column rename if not kept in the writer
- NEW `Services/ExportService+Bid.{h,mm}` (`exportOST` / `exportOSP`), `ViewController+ExportBid.mm`; CHANGED `ViewController+Export.mm` (menu entries `:14-30`), `DatabaseViewController.mm` (bid-row export action, anchor pattern `:805-807`)
- NEW tests: `Config/Tests/Export/test_ost_writer.cpp`, `test_osp_writer.cpp`, `test_export_integrity.cpp`, `test_export_roundtrip.cpp`; `Config/Tests/Import/test_cab_writer.cpp`; `Config/Tests/Fixtures/export_ost_oracle.txt`
- Dependencies: system zlib (`libz.tbd`, already linked), SQLite, `Digest`; no pugixml writer

**Touch adaptation.** Logic (formats, order, naming, quantities) is not adapted. UI: no save dialog on iOS, so write the finished file to `NSTemporaryDirectory` and present the existing `UIActivityViewController` (Save to Files, AirDrop) with the iPad popover anchor; entries in the bid export sheet and as a bid-row action in the project browser; modal progress with the Windows step texts (the frame is "Exporting: <name>..." since Windows d26893c, it was "Exporting <name>..."; the steps keep their text) and a Cancel that removes temp files; background queue + `beginBackgroundTask`; stream rows and 32 KiB CAB blocks (never hold a whole drawing or bid as dict-of-strings: jetsam risk); free-space check before an OSP; ONE DEFERRED read transaction (`begin_read_transaction`, step 2) and a check that no edit is only in memory (Windows flushes deferred persistence first: `export_handler.py:369-370`); resolve a stale absolute `ImagePath` through the `OST/...` suffix under the current Documents folder before calling a drawing missing; temp cleanup after the share and at launch. **Windows texts kept verbatim:** `No Bid Selected` / `Please load a database and select a bid before exporting.` (`export_handler.py:414-426`); `Successfully exported bid to {filename}` (`:475-480`); `Cannot export OST because database references are invalid: ...` (`ost_exporter.py:650-653`); `Cannot export OSP because referenced drawing files are missing: ...` (`osp_exporter.py:77-80`); the progress steps `Building OST` / `Writing metadata` / `Collecting images` / `Packaging archive` / `Collecting <name>` (`osp_exporter.py:62`, `:104`, `:108`, `:115`; `Collecting <name>` at `:161`).

**Dependencies.** No feature blocks it, but rows authored in M1 / M2 (conditions, folders, layers, pages, scale, paste, holes) MUST round-trip, so they are test fixtures here (the Windows-side proof of everything M1 and M2 wrote is only possible from here); M4 annotation authoring depends on the latin-1 rule (N-M3-09); shares `strip_elevation_suffix` with 5.10 and the plumbing with 5.12. Verification tools beyond host tests: `expand.exe`, the `makecab` fixtures, and (a later job, read-only Windows Python) the real `ost_cab` list / extract and `OstImporter._validated_raw_data` in memory.

**Risk.** HIGH. A silently reduced or unimportable file shows up only on Windows; the new CAB writer must satisfy the Windows FDI and the original app with only `expand.exe`, fixtures and an in-memory Windows run as oracles; many easy-to-miss format rules (YESNO -1 / 1 / 0, `%.15f` floats, six-part dates, NULL vs zero-default, escape table, reversed `BidAreaCondition` rows); phone memory and storage limits can kill a large export; Windows itself has silent-loss and unimportable cases that must not be copied; NO real `.ost` / `.osp` sample exists in either repo and the Windows golden file is circular (N-M3-14).

**Size and order of work.** L. Order: (1) `CabWriter` + `expand.exe` / FDI-contract tests; (2) `begin_read_transaction` (with its host test) + `read_raw_bid` + cell formats + attribute-order tables; (3) `prepare_for_export` with the removed-rows report; (4) `OstWriter` + round-trip invariants (iOS import of the iOS export equals the source, except the Employees columns that N-M3-10 scrubs); (5) `OspWriter`; (6) the read-only Windows acceptance run; (7) UI + share; (8) optional drop-elevation stage (last, off by default); (9) device pack rows + the desktop acceptance check of N-M3-14.

**Windows oddities and the safe behaviour kept** (register X-1 to X-8): X-1 `raw_bid_integrity.py:645-728` - rows with a dangling reference are dropped in a loop, no log, no field in the result -> prune like Windows (the file must pass the Windows integrity check) and return the removed rows (table, UID, column, missing UID): "N rows were left out" (N-M3-08). X-2 `ost_exporter.py:598-608` - TAB is not escaped, an attribute TAB reads back as a space by XML normalisation (reasoned) -> also write `&#x9;`. X-3 `ost_serializer.py:25-27` - only NUL is stripped; U+0001-0008, 000B, 000C, 000E-001F and U+FFFE / U+FFFF are not XML 1.0 characters (`Core/Import/XmlCharData.hpp:10`, probed with pyexpat) and the Windows importer rejects the file -> replace by U+FFFD and report the count. X-4 `osp_exporter.py:255-257` vs `osp_importer.py:22-29`, `:81-101` - only nine path characters are replaced: a bid named Con / Aux / COM1 or a drawing named PRN.pdf gives a member the Windows importer rejects -> run every member name through the importer predicate, repair and tell the user (N-M3-13). X-5 `serialization.py:7-8`, `:70-73` - `BidTexts.Name` / `BidCallOuts.Name` are latin-1: one other character fails the WHOLE Windows import -> refuse with row and column named (N-M3-09). X-6 `ost_exporter.py:821-828` - a condition of an unsupported type exports zero `BidAreaCondition` quantities with a log line only -> kept, with a visible "N conditions exported with zero quantities". X-7 `ost_exporter.py:180-198`, `:1050-1054` - referenced employees' login, address, phone, e-mail and password are written into a file meant to be sent -> scrubbed by default (N-M3-10): the UID references, number and name columns are kept, the other columns are written empty (all nullable, `database_creator.py:711-729`) and a host test pins it; carrying them through like Windows is a named exception to the safe-behaviour rule that needs your OK. X-8 `ost_exporter.py:471`, `:575-576` - BidAreas / BidTypAreas / BidTypAreaCounts get an extra empty child element named like the container (the importer skips it) -> reproduce (pinned); the golden attribute-order file is a transcription of the exporter, not an oracle (`tests/helpers/mdb/ost_attribute_order_golden.py:1-4`).

**Texts (owner decision EXP-21, job 11).** All alert, progress and note texts of this feature are the Windows sentences of section 4.15; the writer returns a typed result and the Core text module builds the sentence; no raw error ever reaches the alert.

### 5.12 PDF export with takeoff markup, Bluebeam-compatible (B-004 in `PORTING_BLOCKERS.md`; L, risk HIGH until the QPDF spike passes)

**What Windows does.** One PDF, one output page per selected plan page. Each page is the source PDF page placed as VECTOR content on a canonical page (native Rotate, CropBox and UserUnit normalised, then user rotation and flip applied once) or a blank page (blank when the page has no PDF drawing, its image layer is hidden (`page.layer_visible`) or the show mode hides the original: `pdf_exporter.py:279-301`; M3 applies the layer flag, the show mode joins in M4). On top: all visible takeoffs as Bluebeam `/Polygon` measurement annotations, optional caption text, optional elevation callouts, the 9 visible OST annotation families and the transformed source annotations. A Python mapper (`pdf_exporter.py`, 1244 lines) feeds a C++ QPDF writer (`pdf_writer.cpp` 1162 + `bluebeam_annotation.cpp` 1668 lines).

**Windows modules.**
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/visualization/exporters/pdf_exporter.py:126-247`, `:260-301`, `:553-598` (page info; native-geometry refusal `:559-565`), `:600-893`, `:895-1244` - export loop, canonical page info, background choice, takeoff collection (area with holes, linear band, count shape), colours, captions, callout box, 9 annotation families
- `ost_visualizer/presentation/handlers/export_handler.py:172-315` - valid-page filter, default names, source-overwrite refusal, snapshot, progress, messages
- `cpp_extensions/src/pdf/pdf_writer.cpp:263-341`, `:656-752`, `:769-919`, `:932-1127`, `:1128-1161` - takeoff polygons with `/Measure` and `/VP`, page geometry, canonical page via Form XObject, source annotation copy, merge with page-contract validation
